ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u1	The meeting was resumed. Royal Decree-Law 3/2015 of 22 March, providing for an extraordinary and additional offer of public employment to combat fraud in public services, to boost the functioning of the Administration of Justice, and in implementation of the measures provided for in Law 27/2013 of 27 December, on rationalization and sustainability of the local administration. To present the royal decree-law, the Minister of Finance and Public Administration has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u2	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, today the Government brings to the Congress of Deputies the validation of Royal Decree-Law 3/2015 of 22 March, which sets in motion an extraordinary and additional offer of public employment for this year. The objective of this royal decree-law is to strengthen the fight against fraud in public services, the drive for the functioning of the Administration of Justice, and the implementation of the measures provided for in Law 27/2013 of 27 December on rationalization and sustainability of the local administration. As you know, the Government of the nation has launched since the beginning of the legislature a policy of containment of public expenditure, aimed at reducing in a decisive way the high deficit that we are in at the end of 2011. As is already well known, this policy of the Government, which assumed to have to subject public expenditure to severe adjustments, has been one of the main factors of the economic recovery that Spain is experiencing, a recovery that has as its main characteristics a firm and progressive economic growth, which according to the latest forecasts recently made known by the International Monetary Fund and the Bank of Spain can be located this year between 2.5% and 2.8%, and a job creation that in annual terms is already more than half a million people. One of the measures taken to reduce the deficit was to drastically reduce the incorporation of new staff into the public sector. The State General Budgets approved in the first three years of the legislature stated that there would be no new highs, except in certain sectors which are considered to be priorities for the provision of essential public services, and even in these sectors access to a maximum of 10 % of the replacement rate was restricted. This is undoubtedly the policy that needs to be done in times of serious crisis, especially when one of the factors characteristic of it is a rapid growth of the public deficit, as a result of a sharp decline in income, which was accompanied by a high increase in expenditure. Paradoxically in the toughest years of the crisis, in the previous legislature, as the number of unemployed grew exponentially in the private sector, in the public sector the number of staff increased beyond what is necessary, with the consequent increase in the cost of payroll to the administration. No one could think that the number of people employed in the public sector would increase in the midst of the crisis by more than 400,000 people, but that happened in Spain, and also neglecting sectors that should be priorities, as has been shown later. At present, the Spanish public sector workforce is at levels that we consider to be adequate to provide with guarantees the services that Spanish society needs, so that there is no need to undertake further reductions. What we are doing is to assess which sectors need to be strengthened in order to maintain and improve the quality of the services provided by the Administration to citizens, and to continue working on the main objective of this legislature, which is the exit from the crisis, economic growth, and job creation. The new economic scenario and the need to strengthen certain areas of public administration have led the Government to launch a wider public employment offer than in the first years of the legislature, a public employment offer that does not abandon the philosophy of expenditure control, because a long path of fiscal consolidation is still ahead, but which makes the replacement rate in the essential sectors more flexible up to a maximum of 50%. Moreover, the offer of ordinary employment is accompanied, as last year, by an extraordinary offer of employment in key sectors, which is included in the royal decree-law that we bring to the House. A total of 7,416 new places will be convened in 2015, with an increase of 340 % over last year. In addition, domestic promotion positions increase by 79% and reach 3.834. With regard to the supplementary offers contained in this royal decree-law, I would like to stress that much of the work of the new officials who are going to occupy these places will be focused on the prevention and fight against fraud and crime, an aspect in which the Government has made special efforts in this legislature, and which is undoubtedly a widespread demand in society. Local Government officials. Following the articulation of the royal decree, in the first place it is authorized to call up to 200 places on the scale of local government officials with national authorization. Since 2007, when this competition became dependent on the autonomous communities, the call for these places has been much less than the number of vacancies, even before the restrictions on the offer of public employment began. The situation is that the largest number of vacancies are in the sub-scales of intervention-treasury and secretariat-intervention, which are the areas assigned to the functions of internal control and control of economic-financial and budgetary management, in addition to the function of accounting, treasury, and collection of local entities. These are functions that are increasingly important every day, both for the control of the public deficit and the achievement of budgetary stability and for the fight against fraud and corruption in the field of local government. The Law on Rationalization and Sustainability of Local Government adopted by the Government more than a year ago establishes a new regulation of the legal regime of these officials, extending the powers of the General Administration of the State over them, and giving the Ministry of Finance and Public Administration the power to approve the offer of public employment and the selection of local government officials with national authorization. Last year this ministry already approved the call of 210 additional places to the ordinary offer, so we intend to gradually remedy the deficit of officials accumulated in this field. It should also be borne in mind that in this case the additional offer does not entail an increase in cost, since local corporations where they provide their services must have these positions budgeted, regardless of whether they are vacant or not. The fact that they are not covered by staff on this scale involves the appointment of temporary staff or accidental appointments. The Government believes that strengthening the role and functions of the intervention agencies requires greater professionalism and effectiveness in the exercise of internal control functions through the appointment of qualified staff and technical expertise. Combating tax fraud and expenditure control. Secondly, this royal decree includes the convening of new places in the field of combating tax fraud and expenditure control. A total of 254 places will be allocated to the Tax Agency, and another 40 to the General Intervention of the State. The role of the Tax Agency, both in the voluntary fulfilment of fiscal and customs obligations by citizens and in the prevention and fight against tax fraud, is essential to ensure the solvency of the State and the provision of public services to citizens. We are faced with an essential institution in our country, an institution that has been modernised as few, which works with higher efficiency rates than in most of the countries in our economic environment, and which demonstrates in each of its actions a high degree of professionalism and commitment, for the benefit of the general interest of the Spaniards. Unfortunately, as I said in the case of local officials with national authority, in the years when there were financial means to increase their staff, the staffing of the agency was neglected, although we were then required to do so by the following political leaders, even though we have had to work under essential conditions of budgetary restraint. In order to maintain the agency's operational capacity at levels appropriate to its role during these years, the Government included it among the key sectors when approving public employment offers. In addition, we are working to improve management methods, advance the application of new technologies and introduce measures to rationalize and stimulate productivity, with the aim of improving their effectiveness and efficiency. The consequence of all this has been a clear improvement in control actions, in tax collection on a voluntary basis, and especially in the prevention and fight against tax fraud. The three years of the current legislature have been the best results in the fight against fraud in the recent history of our public finances. In 2014, revenues reached a record 12.3 billion euros, the result of the professionalism and efficiency of its staff, and the guidelines with which they have worked. The fight against fraud has been a constant in these three years, and it will continue to be so from now on. Many people, including some of his ladies and gentlemen, scandalize and attack the Government when information is published in the media about the actions of the Tax Agency, in which even names of people of social relevance appear. What would scandalize me would be that these actions were not done, and that people of social relevance could not be investigated, as has happened on previous occasions. [[Applauds]] This Government and this Minister fully respect the independence and autonomy with which the officials of the Tax Agency work, and intervene only to try to improve the means at their disposal, to support them in their work when they are even attacked from outside, and to stimulate them in their work, which is to fight against fraud and to increase the revenue of the State. They are told that in order to do so they have to go where they detect possible fraud bags or signs of crime, without having to worry as much as possible about the names and surnames of potential fraudsters, their professional activity, their social relevance or their political affiliation. In the fight against fraud and corruption, respect for the law and the general interest are above all; above political or electoral interests, above comrades and rivals, above friends and enemies, and each one responds to his or her actions. [[Greetings.- Mrs. Álvarez-Arenas Cisneros: Very good!]] That is what the Government and this minister are promoting, that is what is causing so many successes in the fight against fraud, and so much information in the media; what they do not understand is because they prefer to close their eyes to reality in order not to recognize that also in this area, as in economics with economic recovery and in so many others, the Government is doing what it must to get Spain forward. The Government considers it a priority to increase human resources in the fight against tax fraud in order to advance the objective of ensuring that all citizens and businesses contribute to State expenditure, according to their income and wealth. As of last year, the Tax Administration increased its staff by 166 new positions in the offer of extraordinary employment, and 72 more in the ordinary, to which more than 600 positions of internal promotion were added in order to strengthen the staff structure of the agency. This year the 254 places provided for by this royal decree-law are added to the 230 included as a replacement rate in the offer of regular employment, which adds up to 484 places. With regard to the general intervention of the State, I would like to point out that in recent years some regulatory changes have been adopted, which will entail an important additional workload and consequently increased staffing needs, which are being addressed in this extraordinary job offer. On the other hand, the royal decree-law that we submit to the validation of the Chamber authorizes the convening of 150 places in the field of the fight against labour fraud and social security. The Government considers it necessary to increase the staff of the Labour and Social Security Inspection system in order to step up the fight against irregular employment and social security fraud. The aim is to be able to step up action in this area in order to put an end to the practices of irregular and submerged economy that rob workers of social rights, leaving them without social security coverage and avoiding the payment of quotas necessary for the maintenance of the system. Increased human resources will enable new inspection campaigns to be planned and the coordination and cooperation tools of the Labour and Social Security Inspectorate with other bodies responsible for labour, social and employment policies to be strengthened. It is also necessary to strengthen other areas, such as the Legal Service of the Administration of Social Security, the General Intervention of the State, and the managing bodies and common services of Social Security. Finally, the royal decree-law authorizes the summoning of 160 places in the body of judicial secretaries, half of them re-entry and the other half of internal promotion. The latest procedural reforms have incorporated greater powers for the secretaries of justice, who as heads of the judicial office are a key step in driving judicial proceedings, and thus in ensuring greater agility, efficiency and efficiency in the resolution of judicial matters. I conclude, ladies and gentlemen, by affirming that after a period of forced restriction on access to the public service, the Government is increasing this year the number of places in sectors that are absolutely essential for the proper functioning of the administration, to equip the public service that citizens receive with greater quality, to improve the income of the State, and as we are demonstrating to strengthen economic recovery and job creation. The extraordinary offer of public employment included in the royal decree-law that we submit to the validation of the House will incorporate in key areas of the Administration new professionals who wish to undertake a career in the public sector at the service of their fellow citizens. The political will of the Government is to strengthen unique areas of action that are closely related to the control and fight against fraud and crime, whether from the point of view of the Administration, from the tax, labour, or in general with the work carried out by the justice system. I firmly believe that all citizens are obliged to comply with our obligations wherever we carry out our work, and especially when those obligations involve participating in the maintenance of the services provided by the State or in the clean and efficient management of the public resources managed by the politicians; and I firmly believe that the State as a whole must put all possible means in place to control and punish fraudulent and criminal conduct, regardless of who the individuals or companies, entities or institutions commit them. From this conviction I ask this House to validate this royal decree-law which strengthens our public administration as the backbone of the State with new human resources, and makes the work of institutions that fight fraud, corruption and all kinds of crimes more effective. Thank you very much, Mr President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u8	Thank you very much, Mr Azpiazu. By Union Progreso and Democracy, Mr Ancholous has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u9	Thank you, Mr. President. By means of this royal decree, the Government carries out an extraordinary call for public employment, in addition to the ordinary one carried out in the General Budgets of the State, and this leads to a first comment. The budgets were adopted recently and have already undergone numerous changes. Every time we discuss them, the Minister of Finance presumes the rigour and efficiency with which he designs them, but one year and another they get outdated, sometimes before they start to run. In this extraordinary call, attention is focused on three areas: the first is the local authorities, for which internal control and control places are called. It's good to see you, welcome. Let us remember that the origin of so many problems in our local administration, such as expenses incurred without being budgeted or invoices in the drawers, have had much to do with the misappropriation of these national officials and their progressive loss of independence. However, the economic memory itself points to something relevant, that these places already exist; they are occupied by interims, but they already exist, it is not a endowment of new resources. This measure would certainly have much greater effect if it were part of something that the Minister of Finance and Public Administration has not done, a thorough reform of the local administration. If this were part of that reform in depth, part of the merger of municipalities - we continue in our country with more than 8,000 municipalities, a large part of them of less than 1,000 inhabitants - of greater size, this type of measures would have more effect and more meaning, instead of remaining in slashes and partial measures, such as these. The second area of action is the administration of justice. Judicial secretaries' places are called. Welcome back, of course, to our collapsed Justice Administration will do you good. It will never be easy to accurately calculate the economic effects of the type of justice we have, as well as those of its slowness on so many business-related issues, and it is only one facet of the problem. Again, it should be pointed out - the economic memory itself says - that we are not talking about new places, but about those already existing, occupied today by interim staff. I repeat, this would make real sense as part of a reform of the Administration of Justice that deals with more fundamental problems, for example, the politicization of justice. The General Council of the Judiciary continues to have the composition it has, and the Progressive Union and Democracy is the only group in this House that has resigned from being in it. These substantive issues are of greater importance than the cuts, the partial measures that are included here. Third area of action: the fight against fraud, taxation and labour. How can we not agree when this should be a great state pact and a national emergency? We are in a country where 22% of our economy, of our GDP, is a submerged economy, and the different studies to estimate the submerged economy - whether through actual use or electricity - all come to similar conclusions. This means that every year 70,000 or 80 billion euros, 7 or 8 points of GDP, are no longer collected in our country. How can we not agree to provide more resources for this fight against fraud? The main criticism is that they are insufficient reinforcements. This royal decree calls a total of 724 places and, according to the EPA, around 3 million people work in our public sector; that is, we are increasing this public force by 0.02%. It doesn't seem like anything spectacular or could have very significant effects. I'm going to focus my comments on the tax agency because it's something we've been discussing throughout the legislature. We have repeatedly criticised the fact that the resources of the tax agency have been reduced in the State's general budgets by a double way: in its budget allocation and with a replacement rate which you say is 10% or 50%, and I reply that it is at least 90% or 50%. That is to say, of the 100 who retire, 10 are replenished in the first budgets or half in the last budgets. When from the opposition we have said this sort of thing from the majority group, from the government, they have refused. But - how curious! - when they rectify they fall into a certain schizophrenia and to justify their rectifications end up giving reason to opposition groups. We find pearls like the following that you would deny if I said them: There has been a serious negative evolution of the staff in the Tax Agency - it does not say UPyD, its official documents say -. This number has usually been around 28,000 and at the end of 2014 it stood at 25,742, more than 2,000 posts are lost than usual. There has been an increase in the average age of this workforce, from 40 to 50, and with such an ageing workforce there is a risk of losing a quarter more by 2020. That's not true, demagogues, populists! Hey, it's your official documents. You, when you rectify, tell some truths. This happens in a country where we have much less resources in the fight against fraud than our neighbouring countries. In France there are 117,000 people in their equivalent to the Tax Agency; in Germany 110,000; in the Netherlands, a country smaller than ours, 29,000, more than in Spain. Can we be surprised after the lack of effectiveness in the fight against fraud by very efficient, competent and professional officials and all the staff of the Tax Agency? No, we can't be surprised, it's a natural consequence of our policies. This has been one of the great mistakes of your policy this legislature, Mr Montoro, because, as your economic memory also says, the most elementary analyses indicate that there is a very important multiplier effect on the Tax Agency: for each euro of expenditure on inspection, in the fight against fraud, 17 are collected; so it has not been a good way to save the resources of the Tax Agency. The second mistake that has multiplied the first - has a multiplier effect - has been to devote less resources to fighting fraud and promulgating a tax amnesty. I would like to remind you of some of our discussions on this issue. Money laundering. We said that in the first debates on tax amnesty. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u17	Thank you, Mr. President. I'll step in from the seat. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u21	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are invited to a table where justice is a fundamental part of the dishes. The first course has to do with something as technical as interpretation and translation in criminal proceedings, and this allows me to make a first assessment of the initiatives that we are going to see today: the right of interpretation and translation - transposition of a directive -, the status of the victim, civil prosecution, amendment of the Organic Law of the Judiciary. In this tumult of initiatives something will be made clear. The only thing that can be made clear and that is already seen in this reform of the aspect of the organic law on the interpretation and translation of criminal proceedings is that of the Government in the area of justice. We could say that throughout this parliamentary term the Government has been interested in justice something very different from what is of interest to citizens, to potential litigants, and also something quite different from what is of interest to legal operators. The Government is interested - also in this initiative - in power and the market, essentially that: power - again the Organic Law of the Judiciary to see who can influence justice and its independence - and the market, because justice for the Government is not a public service, but a method to create new private markets around the right to effective judicial protection that, in our view, is undermined. This very technical initiative makes it possible to see these attitudes of the Government, because the Community directive seeks the reliability in the criminal proceedings of translation and interpretation. It's something as simple as that. The government in this transposition of the directive shows its true face in these matters. First of all, we can say that the government shows its Europeanism in terms of a Community directive, because this Community directive is transposed out of time, with a threat of sanction on the part of the European Union, and not content with it, in the very initiative that transposes the European directive we propose a legal holiday that leaves us completely out of the time of the sanction and extension. Good way to respect European standards! I have heard during the last few days, particularly with regard to Syriza and the Greek Government, that the rules must be complied with. Well, this government complies with the rules in austerity, but when it comes to meeting the deadline for a Community directive which, on the other hand, it does not like, it does not meet the rules even within the deadline. We are therefore late, but the worst is that we are wrong, because this directive, which seeks the reliability in criminal proceedings of translation and interpretation, which has much to do with the right of defence, with effective judicial protection, is once again a failed initiative. The government's own legislative initiative says that in order to implement translation and interpretation - which is theoretically a service that seeks the best quality of justice - there will be no more than one euro. The Government argues that this is done without a budget. Ladies and gentlemen, it is not serious that this restriction should be raised, although it is a minor restriction within what the Government intends in this legal initiative. The two fundamental aspects of the directive were, first, to have a reliable translation, a register of translators and interpreters. Well, the Government considers - it has said so to the rapporteurs - that it is not the subject of this initiative which transposes a directive on the registration of interpreters and translators, the registration. It leaves us unregistered and says that the registration will take place in a subsequent legislative development. Do you see any more absurd than this? The absurdity has not yet ended, because the General Council of the Judiciary itself and the Council of State itself recommend to the Government that for the implementation of this register the most objective is that there be an examination, which in addition have proposed to it all the chairs of the Spanish universities that have to do with translation and interpretation. Neither is there going to be a record, nor is there going to be a test. You will say: we transpose a directive late, we do not contemplate the registration of translators and interpreters, nor does the objective criterion for that registration, which can only be an examination, so why does the government transpose the directive? For a formal enforcement of late and bad transposition and, on the other hand, for an unacceptable legalization of a chapuza, which are sub-contracts in the field of translation and interpretation. Because it is true that the government does not establish registration or examination, but it does establish certain indeterminate aspects - how curious, in justice the indeterminate is the norm! - so that the subcontracts of translation and interpretation, which today do business of such a delicate matter in the defense and in the criminal proceedings of the justice. This is where these subcontracts are carved into the law of the Government. Ladies and gentlemen, in the event that this is not enough, in the event that the Government also establishes a rule whereby, if there is a reason for urgency, this transposition of the directive will not have to be complied with at all, if there is a reason for urgency at last, this law does not apply at all. And as in the Spanish justice system, because of the lack of resources, of means in judicial proceedings, urgency is the norm, in the end the norm has been emptied; the government empties it from the budgetary point of view, from the normative point of view and also empties it with a procedure that is to skip the norm through urgency. That is ultimately the draft that we have to discuss today and the amendments that come from the Senate, which are all technical amendments. We could say: a problem has been saved in the Senate, which has to do with the competition issue, those autonomous communities that have their own language. But it has been saved in a way that eventually turns the law into wet paper. Do you know how they do it? Excluding the translation and interpretation of the right of defence and converting an organic law into a normal law. Ladies and gentlemen, for this trip, we didn't need saddlebags. I'm done. Justice is not the priority of the Government, it is obvious. The Government has two priorities: one is how it interferes with justice and another is how it turns a public service into a private business. But mocking a Community directive and mocking this House, I think it is too much. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u25	Thank you, Mr. President. With the debate on this bill, I must recall a rather anomalous situation that has already taken place in the first debate in this House, and it is that, being a fundamentally technical rule, the vast majority of groups that make up the opposition abstained or voted against this bill, and that was because the disdain and disdain with which the Government had transposed the two directives that this bill is about were so great, that the rest of the groups that we make up this House refused to take part in this demeanor. [[The lady]] The truth is that in a bicameral system like ours, in which we have a second reading chamber such as the Senate, which could often establish the function of amending and resolving those texts that came out of Congress and that did not respond to all the legislative needs they seek, such as the draft in question, there is the circumstance that in this case the Senate has served, if I may say so, for a washing and hairstyle, but for a subject who still has the same face. That means that in this case my group will have to abstain again in a vote on the whole of the draft, which should not initially be the case, because I insist that the need to transpose these two directives is something that is out of the question. Specifically, the Socialist Parliamentary Group is going to vote against two amendments that have been adopted in the Senate. The first refers to the amendment of section 124 of the Criminal Procedure Act. As the speakers who have spoken before me have explained very well, there is a key aspect to this bill, which is the official record of translators and interpreters. In Article 124 you - I refer to the People's Party, of course - continue to refer to the publication of official lists according to the competent administration, when both the directives themselves which are transposed and the nature of this law make it essential to mention the official register of translators and interpreters. For that reason, on a point of congruence with what we argued in the first parliamentary debate on this bill, we will vote against the text of article 124 that comes to us from the Senate. The second amendment that comes from the Senate and to which we will also vote against is the one that refers to the creation of the new final provision first, and we will do so for three reasons. Firstly, because, in view of what is a reference - this time yes - to the creation of an official register of translators and interpreters, what you do is refer to the enactment of a future law or to a future bill within a maximum period of one year to be presented in this House, with the consequent processing that should follow, and this seems to us to be an unacceptable time frame. At the time, we have already proposed that in any case, this House should not be dealing with that bill within a maximum of three months, and we continue to think so. That would be the first reason why we would vote against that final provision. The second reason is that, if what this law really seeks is to guarantee the rights of any person who has to take part in a judicial, procedural or criminal proceeding in our country with a language other than his or her own - let us in case any of us or citizens who may be listening to this debate have to be part of a criminal proceeding in Germany or Italy - I imagine that that person would want to have the maximum possible guarantees with regard to the translation and interpretation of what is explained to him or her and of what she also says. Therefore, the only way we understand that we have to guarantee that right to a defence which undoubtedly assists such persons is that one of the functions of the official register of translators and interpreters is to control the academic training and the qualification of those persons who must be registered, because it is the only way that we can guarantee, as I said, that right which assists them to have a correct translation and interpretation. That should be one of the functions of the register, and we did so or mentioned it in the amendment that we tabled and that you have completely ignored. The third reason why we are not going to vote in favour of this final provision is because, as other speakers who have spoken before me have also commented, you are again incurring a question that could even have a vice of unconstitutionality, such as the fact that you are entering a field in which the autonomous communities have competences, especially those that have transferred jurisdiction of justice, and you are talking about a single register, and that is an issue that, I insist, can lead us to problems. I would like once again to mention that it is clear to me that the rapporteurs of the People's Party, at least in the process of the Congress of Deputies, have made every effort to try to improve the text, but in this case my Socialist Parliamentary Group blames the Government, as we did in the first debate and we do now. Otherwise it would not have been a transposition, this text deserved to be voted against. Thank you. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u26	For the People's Parliamentary Group, Mr Serrano.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u27	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I rise to the rostrum to defend the adoption of the Senate amendments to the draft Organic Law amending the Criminal Procedure Act and the Organic Law on the Judiciary as a result of the transposition of two directives on the right of interpretation and translation into criminal proceedings, entering today the last parliamentary stage to proceed with that final approval in this House. First of all, I would like to point out that what we are finally adopting today is not a global amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act or the Organic Judiciary Act, but only a very specific aspect of both legal texts which affects the right of interpretation and translation in criminal proceedings as a result, I insist, of the transposition of two European directives. During this parliamentary process, some parliamentary groups have sought to go beyond the objective of this directive in order to try to introduce through the back door amendments to aspects of the Criminal Procedure Act or the Organic Law of the Judiciary or even the Criminal Code which had little or nothing to do with the European directives to be transposed. Today we put to the vote the final adoption of that text with the Senate amendments. It is a text that, before entering the amendments, is of particular importance because it strengthens the guarantees of the criminal process, strengthening the Spanish criminal procedure, one of the most guarantors in the world. It is a draft law that, throughout the parliamentary process, has been receiving amendments from various parliamentary groups that have undoubtedly enriched and improved the text, especially from a technical point of view, positive contributions that undoubtedly gave greater technical and legal precision to it. Among the most notable aspects of this draft law, apart from the definition of the guarantees that are established for any person who sees or may be involved in criminal proceedings, we must also highlight the explicit mandate of the Government to create within one year - a period that is in no way to be exhausted - an official register of translators and judicial interpreters, a register that was a demand in the collective and that this draft law, which we are now submitting to its final adoption, reflects in this regard. Let us remember that this law does not regulate such a national registry, but rather announces its creation or rather its regulation within a maximum period of one year, which I insist, is a period that does not have to be exhausted, and it will be that future regulation that develops it, respecting the competition field, of course. I do not know where the contrary can be deduced from, since the fact that it has to be regulated about being registered does not in any way imply that the area of competition is not respected. It must be borne in mind, however, that, in accordance with articles 149.1.5a and 149.1.6a of the Spanish Constitution, the State has exclusive jurisdiction over the administration of justice and procedural legislation. This is the case, whether or not some parliamentary groups like it. The autonomous communities obviously have their powers, but the State too, and therefore respect for the competition principle, must be mutual and reciprocal and not only one-way, as the nationalist parliamentary groups claim. In this regard, we cannot forget something that seems quite reasonable even for a person who can have a thought from the nationalist orbit, and it seems quite reasonable that the creation of an official register of translators, interpreters and experts is also based on the need to guarantee the equal conditions of all citizens in the exercise of their rights and, in the specific case on which we are legislating, to ensure that a basic service for the protection of the right of defence and of the right to a fair trial is carried out with minimum quality requirements throughout the national territory, minimum requirements ranging from compulsory registration, qualification or training requirements, to put just a few examples. Only through a national registration system can it be ensured that the public translation and interpretation service will be provided under the same basic conditions for all citizens throughout Spain. This does not prevent, I insist, the registration cannot be organized territorially or that its subsequent management and the specific provision of the service must be of shared ownership. The existence of an official register of a national character does not imply any violation of the competences of the Autonomous Communities. Therefore, there is no logic to such a fear or suspicion about an issue that we believe is clear, so the political stance of some parliamentary groups on this issue responds more to the fact that they are in permanent electoral campaign than to reality. If the law that will be submitted for final approval refers to the regulation of this registry, it must be that regulation and not that law that governs the operation of it, because such regulation is the exclusive competence of the State and not of the autonomous communities. I shall briefly enter into the specific amendments which we shall be putting to the vote and by procedural economy and which our parliamentary group will vote in favour. The bulk of them responds, on the one hand, as I said at the beginning of my speech, to a technical improvement in the text, both those negotiated with the various parliamentary groups and those presented by our parliamentary group. They are amendments aimed at clarifying or strengthening also the guarantees and the right of defence of suspects or accused persons with sensory disabilities, or as is the fact that the interpreter or translator must keep the duty of confidentiality. On the other hand, another block of amendments aimed at preserving the principle of legal certainty has also been included, especially with regard to setting a certain date for the entry into force of the precepts contained in this law, which, I insist, gives it greater legal certainty. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, and to conclude my speech, I would like to thank the constructive tone and contributions of the vast majority of parliamentary groups to this text, a text that serves to improve the guarantees that surround or may surround a person who may be involved in criminal proceedings. With the Senate amendments that will be put to the vote today for the final adoption of the text, we are giving the green light to a law that is a further step in the European type-approval of the protection of procedural rights in the various countries that make up the European Union and that means a new step forward in this project of success that represents the European Union. Thank you very much, Madam President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u28	Thank you, Mr Serrano. - Draft law on the status of the victim of the crime. [[File number 121/00115]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u29	We turn to the next issue, which is the Senate's amendments to the bill on the status of the victim of the crime. For the defence and fixing of positions, first of all, by the Basque Group, PNV, Mr Esteban.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u30	Thank you, Madam President. We hoped that the Senate process would address a number of issues that were very important to us in terms of the direction of this bill, issues that we have already raised in Congress. They will agree with me that the debate on the status of victims, everything related to them, is often glassy because they seek indirect and interested interpretations of the differentiated political positions of the majority - usually the one supported by the Government - that may have other political groups, either to obtain political revenues or to erode political positions. Our position on this project is based on a calm reflection on the subject and of course on a position of support for the victims, but also on an objective and classical conception of criminal law, because I insist that this is a subject very much given to positions and images. A very short time ago, just when the legal limit for pre-lessons propaganda was over, we have seen how, for example, the President of the Government has gone to place the first stone of the Memorial of Victims of Terrorism in Vitoria-Gasteiz. Well, there is no project or stone because the building is empty; moreover, there is not even the plaque left, because the same, which was by the way monolingual - it did not comply with the bilingual legislation of the Autonomous Community of the Basque Country - disappeared at half an hour. A scuba man went there and took it out half an hour after the president of the government had placed it. An opening of what? The victims, the memorial and the Government's commitment would then be discussed, but that commitment is often a mere image. This project is positive in many areas, but in some others it is criticizing precisely because the Government has been breaking basic concepts in criminal law to look for its image and its propaganda. What do I mean? We are very concerned - we said it already in the process of Congress and we were not the only group - how article 13 of this bill is configured, because in our opinion it attributes to the victims exorbitant possibilities of action that refer to their participation in the criminal proceedings of their victims. According to this article, the participation of the victim in the execution phase, on the one hand, is not bound by the development of any European legislation; on the other hand, we do not know the comparative right in which it arises, as is done in this article 13; and on the other hand, it causes a series of interferences in the Spanish penal and prison system itself. Article 13 creates instruments to condition what the preamble of the law describes as a state monopoly for the enforcement of sentences. Well, that state monopoly is broken, despite what the preamble says, by article 13, because it can seriously compromise, even, the fulfilment of the constitutional purposes attributed to the penalty by article 25.2 of the Spanish Constitution. If the implementation of this project involved more denials of prison benefits, it is clear that it would have a negative impact on the expectations of reinsertion of the sentence and the constitutional purposes of the sentence are set out very clearly, as I have said, in article 25. Moreover, the Council of State itself, when discussing this article, mentions in its report an order from the Constitutional Court of 3 July 1989, in which - I read in text - it is said: the way in which the sentence is served escapes the interest of the accuser in the case from which the sentence derives, insofar as the right to punish, ius puniendi, is held exclusively by the State. One thing is the right to be informed of absolutely all the steps taken by the justice system and the relevant ministry - and that should be perfectly reflected in the law - and another thing is that it can condition the decisions taken by the justice system to the point of urging the prosecution to adopt measures or rules of conduct that the victims themselves consider necessary to ensure their safety, almost privatizing the functions of the prosecution. In short, a clear emphasis is placed on retributive justice in this project and the restorative justice that many victim associations claim is left out of it. Article 15, which speaks of restorative justice, has many conditions spelled out in the text of the law so that that step can be taken. Our amendments sought that this restorative justice could be possible in a greater number of circumstances and in the will of the victims and the victims, but it has not been possible because they have not been dealt with either in the process of Congress or in the process of the Senate. Restorative justice does not constitute any occurrence of this parliamentary group; it is set out in Directive 2012/29 of the European Union. This bill also excludes the victim-victim mediation instrument. On the other hand, in our view, victims'associations are given a privileged position in criminal proceedings that has no parallel in comparative law. Among other things, as we well know, in criminal law the status of victim is closely linked to its status as a natural person and not as an association for procedural purposes; we therefore believe that this wording is also a mistake. We understand that there are some other articles that to effectively protect the victim are interesting, especially in the area of the right to information that victims have and that so far on many occasions it has been obscured when making decisions without being informed at any time. However, the role of conditionality given to victims, especially by looking at retributive justice, breaks our view of the fees of criminal law, and not only that, but also of the constitutional principles set out in article 25 of the Constitution. Furthermore, the competence of the autonomous communities is not preserved so that the regulatory development of the law can leave room for the autonomous communities with jurisdiction in the field of justice to regulate, for example, the functioning of justice services to support and assist victims. This should be possible and, as it is drafted today, the law does not make it possible. Another example is the implementation of protocols for the training of professionals, which should be carried out by the autonomous communities themselves. As a final conclusion, I will say that the amendments accepted in the Senate little or nothing incorporate into the draft. We welcome the amendment made in article 14 to the effect that the victim must be economically compensated first rather than the State and that preferential option is made. However, the rest of the modifications that are made in some articles are practically mere grammatical or style corrections that do not go beyond. Therefore, let us disagree in some parts of this law from a position of defense of the victims, but also orthodox with the fulfilment of the constitutional principles and the canons of criminal law. We express our very clear discrepancy with Article 13.

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u37	Thank you, Mrs. Díez. By the Plural Left, Mr. Llamazares.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u38	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, thirteen amendments have been tabled, all of them technical corrections, which in our view neither substantially nor relatively modify the text of the proposal for a law that was already coming out of the Congress of Deputies. We are discussing, ladies and gentlemen, as other speakers have said, an important debate, that of the status of the victim, but also contradictory, because there are other actors; the victim is very important, but there are other actors in the criminal proceedings. For many, the absence of a victim's status in our country has been offset by the fact that the victim is involved in criminal proceedings, which is much broader in Spain than in our environment, whether as a private accuser, as a popular accuser, as a civil actor, institutions that do not exist in our judicial environment. Apart from this, the truth is that, paraphrasing Reyes Mate, the victims have come to this process to stay, and they must be made hollow. Our parliamentary group has defended a worthy presence of the victims in the criminal process, but not an omnipresent presence; a balanced and balanced presence with regard to the classic subjects of the accusatory process, the judge and the accused; a presence respectful of the rights and guarantees of the offender. That presence, indeed, requires important regulation - complicated, in our view - in a process designed in terms of remuneration, hence the advantage of the introduction of intra-judicial criminal mediation. The victim's intervention in the trial until the sentence must give him all the protagonists that deserve the damage and pain suffered, no doubt. But, once the fair trial has been followed, the sentence has been handed down, which can also be appealed even without having previously been personified in the proceedings, and the enforcement has become firm, the avatars should remain the exclusive power of the State, in order to seek and ensure its exercise, without losing sight of the constitutionally recognized purpose of sentences and measures of deprivation of liberty, which is nothing other than that of the reinsertion of the sentenced person. Victims have the right to be informed and protected in the execution process, but this cannot be extended to the right to determine whether or not a permit, step-by-step or any other legal situation affecting the execution of the sentence, which is the power of the State, is applicable. In our view, excessive intervention at this stage may compromise the fulfilment of the constitutionally recognized purposes of custodial sentences and security measures, the resocialization of the convicted person, which challenge and link the State, society and not individuals. In this sense, references to restorative justice in relation to a criminal law, which transposes a European directive dealing with criminal mediation, can only be seen as positive. However, the bill maintains a timid and restrictive view of restorative or restorative justice. The draft law legalizes the principles of mediation, such as voluntariness, impartiality and, in particular, confidentiality, however it establishes requirements for carrying out mediation which, in our view, are restrictive: obvious ones, which are not prohibited, and imprecise ones, which do not entail risks to the safety of the victim. It seems that there is an unjustified fear that mediation will cause further moral damage to the victim, a revictimization, without repairing that sometimes an excess of protection can lead precisely to the revictimization that is to be avoided. I would like to mention, ladies and gentlemen, two articles which for us have come to tarnish this victim's status without any justification. Article 13, which our parliamentary group has amended in this House and in the Senate, essentially proposes that victims should have the right to intervene in the regime for the enforcement of custodial sentences in a two-way manner. For us, in order to challenge certain judicial decisions in the execution phase, the order by which the prison supervisory judge authorizes the classification of the sentenced person to a third degree before half of the sentence is extinguished, the order granting prison benefits, the parole order and, on the other hand, the order also legitimizing the request that certain measures or lines of conduct be imposed on the parolee. This is a precept rigorously questioned by the Council of State in its report, which calls it questionable and invites the pre-legislator to consider not using this legislative option. The reasons for the deletion of article 13 are as follows: firstly, that the directive does not oblige it; secondly, that there is no support in the jurisprudence of our Constitutional Court or of the European courts, but quite the contrary; thirdly, that the statement of reasons does not provide a justification for this measure, a measure extending the legitimacy of the victims and contrary to the provisions of the Organic Law of the Judiciary which restricts it to the prosecutor's office and to the inmate or conditionally released, who must, in any case, have legal assistance. In our opinion, it limits the observance of the principle of legality in the execution phase, which is a function assumed by the prosecutor's office and comes to interfere with the tasks of the prosecutor's office. Moreover, it compromises the rules and practices of prisons that have not been questioned by anyone since 1979 in this respect, either by doctrine, by jurisprudence or by the Administration. The protection due to the rights and interests of victims, the trust and collaboration of victims in the criminal justice system is not achieved by giving them missions that in a State governed by law are the responsibility of other State bodies specifically created for this purpose, nor by generating unnecessary burdens or immoderate expectations. That trust, which the pre-legislator wanted, was to care for her, listen to her, inform her, protect her and, finally, accompany her and support her on the road to the recovery of the balance lost as a result of the crime, not to generate such distortions or expectations. With regard to article 35, in our view, it is a distortion of the Gender Violence Act, which also discourages complaints in persons affected by gender violence, such as the return of aid to victims of violence who do not receive a conviction from their aggressor; a return which, in our view, is clearly excessive and clearly dissuasive and which has led the equality movement, the feminist movement, to reject article 35. These reasons, ladies and gentlemen, are those relating, on the one hand, to the agreement with the statute; but, on the other hand, the reservations to article 13 and article 35, which are, in our view, unnecessary and excessive, will lead us to abstain in relation to the amendments that come from the Senate, although we are aware that they are not substantial amendments and that they are essentially corrections to errors. Thank you very much.

It includes the direct victim but also indirect victims, such as relatives or assimilations. So, beyond the majority who finally receive the project, we can be satisfied with the work that has been done in this House, in the Committee on Justice, and in the Senate. The vast majority that will support this rule gives maximum democratic legitimacy to the status of the victim, a statute that contains a comprehensive catalogue of procedural and extra-trial rights, as has been said, of all victims of crime in a broad sense, including indirect victims and all victims of the very various crimes, further distinguishing the treatment that we have already been able to create for some of them, such as those of terrorism and gender-based violence, as well as protecting all particularly vulnerable groups. I conclude by reiterating our appreciation for the cooperation of the House, the technical services and all those who have allowed us today to have, without doubt, the legitimate pride of having a more just society with which we suffer the crime, thus becoming a more advanced and more supportive country. Addressing the victims of the crime, which is an obligation incumbent upon us all, will no longer be an option, but an obligation inherent in a State governed by law, a State that recognizes those with special suffering before the crime. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u49	Thank you, Minister of Justice. - DRAFT ENTERPRISE FINANCING LAW. [[File number 121/000119]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u50	We move on, as amended by the Senate, to the bill to promote corporate finance. In order to establish a position, first of all, for the Group of the Progressive Union and Democracy, Mr Anchuelo has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u51	Thank you, Madam President. I have already had the opportunity to discuss this law in depth on behalf of my group in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs, and also to set our position there; but I have also had the opportunity to do something less habitual, which is to incorporate various amendments from my group into the text of the law. I would therefore like to begin by thanking the spokesmen of the majority group - giving concrete thanks to Mr López Garrido and Mr Martínez-Pujalte - for the receptivity they have had to the amendments of the opposition, at least to those of my parliamentary group, in this particular case. That attitude of collaboration, rather than the imposition of the roller, is something that benefits us all. It benefits the 47 million citizens who have to comply with the laws that come out of here, so they should come out in the best version possible, and it also gives more meaning to the work that we all do in the long debates in the Commission and making amendments. As you will recall, the purpose of this law is very important. Our economy is a highly banked economy, that is, credit depends heavily on banking. The usual form of financing, especially for SMEs, is only one: bank credit. That is why the law intends to correct this in a double plane. Firstly, since financing is primarily banking, it seeks to improve access to bank financing. In our opinion, this is where this law limps more and where it could have been more ambitious. However, it incorporates some interesting developments and, in particular, I would like to highlight one: from now on, when there is a significant reduction in a customer's credit, banks will have to notify you beforehand. In this sense, thanks to an amendment by the Progressive Union and Democracy and another by Convergence and Union, this warning has been improved, because the autonomous and micro-symes will also receive these warnings. We'd have liked to go a little further. We proposed that, when the credit is going to be significantly reduced, the customer who is going to suffer that reduction could make claims, even if they were not binding, explaining their situation, but in any case it is a significant advance. The second group of measures of the law seeks to diversify financing so that it is not so dependent on banking. That's where it's newer. Among the alternatives envisaged are participatory funding platforms, crowdfunding. It is a modern and innovative form of financing regulated by law. Thanks to an amendment by the Progressive Union and Democracy we believe that the operations of these platforms will be significantly facilitated. In the original version of the law it was not possible to use as a guarantee in such operations any type of housing or real estate, with the laudable purpose without doubt of protecting the usual housing. With the amendment that we present only the usual housing cannot be used as a guarantee but other types of real estate, which will facilitate such operations. We would have liked to make the life of these participatory platforms even easier, so that small platforms do not have to disburse such high minimum capital. We believe that investor protection could have been improved, because it is considered as an accredited and practically professional investor for any natural person who earns more than 50,000 euros, which is a threshold that we consider to be little protective of those customers. The third and final step that I would like to highlight is the result of a work of the Progressive Union and Democracy, since the beginning of the legislature, in collaboration with the People's Parliamentary Group, which up until now has been translated into non-legislative proposals, and now has legal status. It is the effort to make the complaints services of the Banco de España and the CNMV more effective. These services receive thousands of complaints each year from users of financial services and, when resolutions are in favour of the complainant, that often has no practical consequences. In the law one begins to walk in the direction that this ceases to be so. I say you start walking because it would take more concrete action and you have to put this into practice, but it is a significant step forward. They are no longer non-legislative propositions, it is a legal text that captures - although generically - the need to move in that direction. Senate amendments do not significantly change the text that came out of Congress. We will position ourselves on them in the individual vote of each, but with the comments made it is clear that we maintain our overall positive assessment of this law. Thank you very much, Mr President. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u52	Thank you, Mr. Anchovielo. For the Group of the Plural Left, Mr Garzón has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u53	Thank you very much, Madam President. I speak briefly because we in the Economics Committee had an opportunity to discuss the depth of the bill. Among other things and very fundamentally we said that it was not a bad bill, that is, that it was not necessarily bad, but that it was clearly left in an insufficient instrument to correct the problems of the economy. We understand that the lack of financing for small and medium-sized enterprises is one of the factors that explains one of the problems of the Spanish economy. The European Central Bank states that there are three surveys that it usually does: the first is that small and medium-sized enterprises do not find customers; the second, financing; and the third, the level of wages, which oppress the ability to benefit; and in addition, in that order. What is surprising is that in this legislature, being the problems that I have just mentioned from the beginning to now and in the same hierarchy, the People's Party Government has concentrated only on the third of the factors mentioned here, that is to say the wage level, which was also fundamentally affecting large companies; however, it has left aside both the lack of demand - in fact the policies have increased that problem - and the lack of funding. It was attempted through financial rescue, at least formally, to correct that problem, that is, to reach financing for small and medium-sized enterprises, and after so many years of trying to make financial reforms that had already begun with the Socialist Party in the Government, we witnessed that this problem had not been solved and, instead of addressing the fact that alternative solutions would have to be sought, it had been preferable to continue making bills that did not follow the root of the problem. This is one of those bills, it has no prejudice, but it does not really serve to address that very important problem that we face. The correlation of forces in the Senate also, that is, the absolute majority of the People's Party, means that in essence the amendments that have come from the Senate have not changed anything at all and that we have to reiterate our assessment of the bill with the amendments already added. After all, large companies have been able to find new financing, because it is a scale economy that allows them access to financial markets, which allows them access to a number of financial instruments that a small and medium-sized neighborhood company will never be able to do. However, this bill attempts to enable instruments, such as securitisation, for small and medium-sized enterprises to imitate the behaviour of large companies, when that will be literally impossible and, one of the most damaging effects it will have, is that a new financial bubble is being fed by repeating the mistakes that led to the international financial bubble and the real estate bubble. That is why, in our view - it was one of the fundamental amendments that we tabled in the Commission and which we were rejected - it is necessary to create a public banking centre, that is, a financial instrument which now has clear objectives of financing small and medium-sized enterprises in addition to those with productive transformation objectives. In other words, not only a single measure, the creation of the public bank, but a pack where this measure was inserted and which would also do so by taking advantage of the current situation in which much of the financial system has been nationalized and could be used for this issue. However, it has been decided to dispense for political reasons - eminently political, not technical in any case - to give preference to other totally non-existent formulas, such as crowdfunding. Crowdfunding is a tool that I believe no one has the capacity to oppose because it is not perverse, but it is not the solution nor can it be imagined that crowdfunding is accessed in the same way when it is a political party, when it is a business project of billions of euros, in front of a small business project in a neighborhood, which by far crowdfunding that it does not have the capacity to have that accumulated money allow it to move forward. Not to mention the asymmetry in information: What information does that person who enters the Internet and finances a business project have, in the face of the classic alternative of an existing savings bank, a financial system, a financial institution, with its managers, its technicians who know the field, the problem, experts in the field and with the ability to discern whether it is profitable or not and, therefore, with greater guarantees of making a good decision? Faced with this model, which was the existing one, and which had to be modified because it had enormous problems, mainly because it had turned to banking, the People's Party presents us with a bill where crowdfunding, a deeply abstract issue, can solve and replace that mechanism. He's basically naive. We are dealing with the umpteenth draft law to solve the problem of corporate financing and we will be attending in the coming months to new bills that will say, again, that come to solve this problem. It does not go to the root, which is in the financial system, in the financial system of large companies and in the only possible alternative, which is a good public banking design that has very clear political and technical objectives of how the Spanish economy is to be made and how it wants to distribute that power among the different subjects of the Spanish economy: large companies, small and medium-sized enterprises, consumers. Until that is done, we will face a desideratum and will be able to benefit some specific economic units, with names and surnames, practically, but will not be able to benefit the Spanish economy as a whole. We are therefore going to abstain in the vote on all the amendments, with the exception of one, to which we will vote against, because we believe that there is a contradiction in expressing a new autonomy for the National Securities Market Commission so that it can choose, without having to obey the principles of public offer, the contract of the templates. As it seems to us that there is no place in this bill and that there is no room for contradiction, we are going to vote against article 94. As for the rest, we're going to abstain. Thank you very much.

A very deep crisis, as has been said here by other speakers, which has affected businesses and, above all, small and medium-sized enterprises, which are the paradigmatic example of our productive fabric, since we have so many of that nature. This type of business had only one access to financing, which is banking financing. What the Spanish economy must aspire to and what we must all contribute to is to ensure that there is the best possible way for such financing, especially for it to be won in dimension. That is, until our micro-enterprises gain a little more in size we will hardly be able to get out of this crisis definitively, and that would be achieved with a good financing model. Indeed, the reports of the European Union, of the European Central Bank, continue to indicate that our companies have more difficulty in accessing finance than the average of the European Union. Funding remains segmented, although it is certainly improving. However, it remains segmented, and it would therefore be essential for this problem to be resolved. There are many companies with viable schemes - Mr Sánchez i Llibre said - which could continue to work perfectly on the market, and whose problem is not their lack of viability or of possibilities for operation, but the lack of funding. We should therefore try to eliminate sources of privilege or excessive imbalance in favour of the financial sector. As the spokesman for the People's Group said earlier, the entry into force can be delayed to eight months, and since the law incorporates some of the positive elements, we should shorten, shorten this period of entry into force to the maximum, and for this reason it is all inconsistent with the need to improve the flow of finance to SMEs. We regret that some amendments by the Socialist Parliamentary Group aimed at forcing the banking institution not to reduce the flow of financing, i.e. the financing of the institutions for a minimum period of one year, and under the conditions under which the company had already granted the credit, have not been included if the creditworthiness of that small and medium-sized company is demonstrated. This is not guaranteed and I would have liked it to have been so. We also proposed to substantially reduce the time limits for the entry into force of the right to notice by a maximum of two months, due to the termination or reduction of the granting of credit for SME financing. As we say in the current text we can reach eight months, and that is too long, which is therefore inconsistent with the urgent need to improve bank financing for small and medium-sized enterprises. We intended to increase the minimum deadline to four months for the bank to notify its intention not to extend the credit to a SME, which would also benefit small and medium-sized enterprises. We would also like to make clearer the inclusion of the self-employed within the scope of the rights of SMEs in cases of cancellation or reduction of the flow of funding. We also intended to reduce the minimum amount to 25% of the total amount of financing, by which the bank has to notify the advance notice in order to reduce the flow of financing or the financing granted by the SME. Finally, we have put a lot of emphasis on a need that the Spanish model continues to have, such as the need to promote financing for the productive economy through a public investment bank. We are not talking about a public bank in general, but yes - let's put the name aside - about a powerful, muscular instrument that allows investment to be promoted. We have various instruments, ICO, Cofides, Cesce, CDTI, which are quite scattered, and probably an entity that would bring them all together and that could be considered a public investment bank, following the French or Israeli model, would be very positive especially for the start of productive activities: startups, new economic activities, etc. This is something that we still lack and our amendment defends it, also claiming that it is governed by a business model based on efficient management of public resources, and that it is subject to the principles of objectivity, transparency, good governance, and accountability. Probably with these elements the law would have been more effective or had a faster impact, but it has not been possible. In any case, it clearly incorporates positive aspects. We are going to vote, I repeat, in favour of the amendments that come from the Senate, although we continue to point out this inadequacy of elements that would have been essential for a rapid exit from this tremendous crisis that has suffered the Spanish economy and society.
