ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u58	Thank you, Mr. Caldera. People's Parliamentary Group. Mr. López Garrido.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u59	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I will leave the thanks for the end because they will also be express and very sincere thanks, and I say this from the heart. I believe that the tone of the speeches has only picked up the tone of the work, the tone of the development of the legislative process in our Committee on Economic Affairs, in this Congress, and what has certainly happened also in the Senate, and that has been very clearly perceived, so much so that almost, because of the way the speeches have taken place, I was tempted to leave part of my speech, since with different nuances of speech from the parliamentary groups, they have been explaining what this law is, and they have been contextualizing it. In any case it is necessary for me to make this context, although it is true that other speakers have referred to this. The economic crisis from 2008 began to feel very strong in our country. It is undoubtedly the longest, deepest economic and financial crisis that has left the greatest mark on our society and our recent history. This economic crisis immediately resulted in a lack of financing for our companies, especially because, although credit was being increased to the country's large companies, the fact is that the thousands of small businesses and the autonomous professionals of our country met overnight with the financial flow closing down to them. The worst thing is that when the financing was closed, the viability of the company was also closed, the company was closed, and in just four years 3.5 million citizens went on to increase the lists of unemployment. Behind each of them there was - and still is - a personal, family and social drama. So the concern of the Government and this parliamentary group that supports it was from that first moment of the inauguration, at the end of 2011 and the start of the march in 2012, to put in place measures aimed on the one hand at balancing public accounts. We knew that we had to regain international credibility and that we had to stop spending more than we did in our public administrations, but at the same time - and it is good to make a little historical journey from the Ministry of Economy and Competitiveness - other measures that were urgent and necessary to solve the problem that our financial system had. It needed to be restructured and remediated, and a further series of more specific legislative measures had been incorporated to provide financing for small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as micro-enterprises and self-employed workers. Examples of this evolution of legislative provisions were the Cashier Act or the Venture Capital Entities Act, and we have gradually been facing this necessary change from this House, a necessary change that is of financial culture. I insist that it is a change of financial culture. Other speakers have referred to this. It is not just a question, as has been done through these legislative provisions, of improving the governance models of our financial institutions or of also improving the transparency of these financial institutions, of protecting their customers, the tax collectors, those with current accounts and savings cards, of also protecting taxpayers through the resolution mechanisms - we will soon also have an in-depth debate on this issue here - but it is basically also - and other speakers have referred to this - of changing the majority financing systems of our small and medium-sized enterprises or of our companies in general. It has been said - and I will repeat this - that in our country more than 90% of the funding that our small and medium-sized enterprises receive is bank financing. Some might think that this is common in the rest of the countries around us, but it is not. In other countries of our environment, on the contrary, more than 80% of the financing that companies receive, and especially small and medium-sized enterprises, does not come from banking credit, but from the participation of people who have an interest in investing in specific projects, who want to risk, invest and boost these projects, and in this way they finance entrepreneurs and entrepreneurs, people who have a project to develop and who directly capture this capital from these investors. That's the case in the most dynamic economies in the world, that's the way to feed. That's why participatory funding platforms have recently emerged, and that's why it was and is necessary to regulate these participatory funding platforms, which is popularly known as crowdfunding. This massive dependence on the financial sector caused the crisis in that sector to also result in the closure of many companies that would have been in a position to continue, which had customers, projects and viability, but which did not have the credit to move forward. It is therefore imperative that we address the regulation of these new funding mechanisms. This law on the promotion of business finance, which we discussed today on its return from the Senate, particularly affects, on the one hand, the protection of those small and medium-sized enterprises that saw how surprisingly they were left without credit, without funding, and protects them, since it sets mandatory notice periods, and also comes very in line with the regulation of this new form of financing of business projects through crowdfunding or participatory financing platforms. Reference has been made to incorporations - I have already done so - with regard to this necessary advance notice for small and medium-sized enterprises. We speak very expressly in the law, dear Mr Caldera, of micro-enterprises and self-employed professionals. It's easy to find, I think in your article 2. It is said that, when the flow of financing closes, banks have an obligation to give free credit history to entrepreneurs, so this entrepreneur can address another financial entity with its credit history, with its defaults if there are them, with their compliance, with credit lines, and with associated insurance - it was incorporated through an amendment in this House - in order to be able to find financing elsewhere. Turning already to that second part which has also been extensively referred to by others, which are the platforms for participatory financing, I would like to explain to you what the platforms for participatory financing are. According to the literal tenor of the law, authorized companies are the ones whose activity is to contact professionally, and through websites or other electronic means, a plurality of natural or legal persons who offer financing in exchange for a financial return with other persons who apply for financing for, on their own behalf, a participatory financing project, which are called promoters. The key to this issue are web pages, new technologies, which brokerage takes place through a technology platform. That is already happening in our country. It is therefore necessary to regulate it. We can't live from behind to reality and we had to regulate it. The law is prudent, because this is a new phenomenon, and the law has to be prudent in terms of investor protection and in terms of the functioning of these participatory financing platforms. That's why they're put in conditions. And the law comes back from the Senate improved. These are not just technical improvements. The Senate's amendments - reference has been made by some speakers - incorporate these new advisory services that can provide these participatory platforms in terms of marketing, advertising or design. It incorporates greater security and solvency through these forms, either from actual disbursement or, as Mr Sánchez i Llibre said, from new endorsement formulas or a combination of liability insurance. It is stipulated that the information is accessible, it is incorporated in a Senate amendment. The requirement is that the platform ensure that the customer can make decisions and knows the risks. Therefore if the law was good - the tone of the interveners has manifested it - the Senate is further improved, so recognition is necessary. He said he was leaving my thanks for the end, and I'm going to do it. I am grateful to all the parliamentary groups, and especially at this time Mr Anchuelo, for the deference and generosity he has had in mentioning me and Vicente Martínez-Pujalte in this rostrum. I would like to thank you expressly for your mention. But also to all other parliamentary groups because they have incorporated good things into the bill, incorporated ideas, and significantly improved the law, and in the tone of the interventions was seen. My express thanks also to those who have collaborated with me in the presentation of this law, to Alejandro Fernández and Celso Delgado. I ended by saying that just over a year ago Celso Delgado, in an intervention regarding the Chambers of Commerce Act, referred to the one hundred and twenty-eight years since the adoption of the Royal Decree of 9 April 1886, in which, at the impulse of Minister Montero Ríos, the chambers of commerce, industry and navigation were set in motion in our country. He made a reference from the historical perspective, a certainly profound reference and recognition of the work that the chambers had developed. I hope, by regulating today this new form of financing for our small and medium-sized enterprises and our self-employed workers, within one hundred and twenty-eight years someone will remember us. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u82	Very well; that will be done, Mr Azpiazu. Thank you very much. Excuse me, Mr. Hook, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u83	Thank you, Mr. President. This organic bill amends two of our country's main economic laws, namely the Budget Stability Act and the Autonomous Financing Act. It modifies them minimally; it modifies them only so as to adapt them to the new liquidity mechanisms. This raises two types of problems for my group. Firstly, we do not share a part of the few amendments that have been made; and secondly, we believe that this would have been a good opportunity to amend these two laws much more thoroughly, both of which are in need of reform. I'll start with the Budget Stability Act. I would like to recall that my group voted in favour of it, despite the fact that it seemed to us to be a rather decaffeinated law. It was not exactly our model that was designed there, but we believed and continued to believe that it filled an important gap in our institutional design that was the need to better coordinate the different public administrations of our country. However, this necessary law, which we are voting for, has a problem that is not in its content but in its lack of application. An already somewhat decaffeinated law has not been applied with the ambition with which it could be applied. It has not been applied automatically or transparently, when there have been breaches by autonomous communities in particular or by municipalities. This is not a subjective opinion of me or my group, it is something that the Independent Fiscal Accountability Authority says, or it is something that the European Union itself corroborates when it drafts reports on Spain. These institutions are surprised that not all the mechanisms provided for by law are used in cases of non-compliance. It is something that also confirms reality itself. A few weeks ago we learned about the public deficit data of the autonomous communities and of 17 communities, thirteen failed to meet their deficit target - 17 or 13 - and, in addition, eight worsened their results compared to the previous year. However, the law does not seem to be being applied with all its possibilities. Instead of doing that, new zero-type financing inventions are designed to aggravate the problem in an autonomous election year. Again, I mean opinions of the AIReF that, of course, we share. Some of our amendments have to do with this. The few changes to the Budget Stability Act make this problem worse if it is not used automatically and transparently. There are some changes that have been introduced that will increase the obscurantism in law enforcement, the lack of transparency. For example, so far the adjustment plans and their timetable for implementation have been public and with the changes introduced in this law now the Ministry of Finance will publish information on the adjustment plans. Related information. It is clear that the transparency of the adjustment plans and the timetable for implementation have so far been distorted; so far the information submitted by an Administration when an adjustment plan was in force was generally known, and this is also changed in law. Therefore, these are changes that we do not share and our amendments call for leaving the law on these issues as it was before. There is another issue that we do not share either. We are very pleased, of course, that the principle of withholding resources from the autonomous financing system has been introduced into Spanish legislation, which allows the State to retain, when an autonomous community does not comply with something important, part of the resources with which it is financed. I would like to recall that this is one of the many issues that Union Progress and Democracy put into the political debate many years ago and at that time it was said to be unconstitutional, that we were Jacobins. We are pleased that this principle has been introduced, but once introduced, why not include in it issues such as the debts that the autonomous communities have with the municipalities because of delegation of powers or partnership agreements? I say this because they are important departures from the social spending of the municipalities and on many occasions the communities accumulate debt with the municipalities. If we want to finance social spending, this is one way. These are issues related, as you can see, to the minimal changes that are introduced into the law. But, as I recalled at the beginning, although the Progressive Union and Democracy voted in favour of the Budget Stability Act, this was not exactly our model. Our model was more ambitious and we believe that the past years have given reason to an important key to our alternative model, such as the need to strengthen previous controls. Between taking action, once there has been non-compliance and avoiding non-compliance, it is clearly preferable to prevent, avoid non-compliance, have more prior controls. We proposed at the time three previous control orders. Firstly, greater control over expenditure by all public administrations. In Spanish legislation, control remains, above all, indirect. The public deficit is controlled, but that does not allow expenditure to be controlled because an Administration can inflate its expected revenue and continue to spend more; it is better to control expenditure directly. Today there are controls and expenditure limits in the different administrations, but in the territorial administrations they are self-imposed limits. Would it not be better to adopt them in a coordinated way rather than being self-imposed limits and to have more emphasis on direct expenditure control rather than deficit control? Second preventive measure. Once these expenditure limits have been established, the composition of expenditure is important. If communities have to deal with health and education services, it does not seem to meddle in their autonomy that there is a minimum of expenditure that will have to be spent on these issues in order to ensure that these services are of the same quality throughout the national territory. Therefore, within this expenditure limit, a minimum expenditure on health and education should be established, so that in the event of cuts, other expenditure rather than such essential expenditure should be prioritized in the face of cuts. Third question. We believe that the draft autonomous budgets before they are discussed in the Autonomous Chambers must have prior supervision. This may scandalize, but it is exactly the philosophy of the European Semester within the European Union. If such a philosophy is possible between sovereign partner states in the Union, will it not be possible between autonomous communities of the same country? We think so. Other amendments sought to learn from past experience since the adoption of the Budget Stability Act. When this law was passed, there was no AIReF. By the way, we in an amendment proposed to create an independent tax council but it was not approved. Subsequently, this AIReF was created and this reform of the Stability Act was a good opportunity to adapt it to the existence of the AIReF and to give it more prominence in the calculation of non-observable but very important variables, such as the structural deficit or the medium-term GDP growth rate. They are unobservable variables, but at the center of the law an independent authority would have to have more prominence than it has. By the way, it should also have more prominence in this House when we discuss the General Budgets of the State. The Bank of Spain appears but the President of the AIReF does not appear. Another thing we have learned from experience. You said in the law that by 2020 we would target a public debt of 60% of GDP. It would be desirable to do so, but we are five years away from 2020 and public debt is close to 100 per cent of GDP. I mean, if you don't change what the law says, we're saying we're going to cut public debt 40 points of GDP, 400 billion euros, in five years. That does not seem to be feasible, therefore, as we pointed out in one of our rejected amendments, I do not quite understand why in the Commission this reform of the law should be used to adapt the letter to reality. Other amendments relate to the Autonomous Financing Act. I will expand on this less because we have discussed this issue often in the House, but I remind you that my group believes that the reform of the Autonomous Financing Act cannot be left for the next parliamentary term, and this would therefore be a good opportunity to undertake such reform. Our broad lines of reform are well known: we believe that the key idea in autonomous financing must be the same per capita financing. That is true, inhabitant of adjusted population; adjusted taking into account the demographic differences - greater ageing - and geographical - greater dispersion - that exist between communities. With the same per capita funding, similar essential services - health, education - can be guaranteed with the same quality throughout Spain. We believe that it is contrary to that principle of equality that the outsourced regimes, as calculated, cause such enormous over-financing in the outsourced communities and we defend the unity of the Tax Agency and that it should not be fragmented territorially because doing so would reduce their efficiency in the fight against fraud and increase their costs. These are the amendments that we defended in the Commission and which were rejected and which we are bringing back to the plenary waiting for any of them to be taken into consideration. Thank you, Mr. President.

To this end, an electronic register of conventions is created, which will allow monitoring of the agreements signed and in force between communities and municipalities. In this way, if there is a breach of the payment obligations laid down in these agreements, the State may act to ensure that the injured city council covers. What they can't do is delegate it and load the mochuel to the suffocated local arks and, on top of it, don't pay, because in the end they collapse the services. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u100	Thank you, Mrs Romero.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u101	The next item is the debate on all legislative initiatives. First, the debate on the bill amending Act No. 43/2003 of 21 November 2003 on Montes. The Minister for Agriculture, Food and the Environment has the floor for her presentation.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u102	Thank you very much, President. I have the honour to appear before this House to introduce the bill amending Act No. 43/2003 of 21 November 2003 on Montes, pursuant to article 149.1.23 (a) of our Constitution, which confers on the State exclusive competence in basic legislation on mountains, forestry and environmental protection. This bill amends aspects of the existing law that could improve and integrates new European standards that emerged during these years. Some problems, such as fragmentation of forest property, which is highly atomized in some regions, result in a lack of economic profitability in forest activity. This leads to the abandonment of the mountains with the negative consequences that result from it. This has not been sufficiently resolved under the current law. The high economic and administrative costs, as well as the complexity of the management and planning of our mountains, lead to their abandonment and to the possibility of being transformed into potential fuelwood warehouses that can burn in the face of extreme weather conditions, as we unfortunately see every summer. Mountains are a fundamental part of the environment and an essential part of the rural environment and the treatment of the forest sector must be comprehensive and inclusive, as the problems affecting mountain management also affect the rural environment, affecting the population living there. It is therefore necessary to improve the national legal framework for the forestry sector in order to achieve better managed, more cared for and more protected mountains for the benefit of our environment, including the wealth and employment of people living in rural areas. The purpose of this bill is to optimize the social, economic, environmental and cultural functions that characterize the mountains and that the forestry sector optimizes its contribution to the sustainable development of the economy. The main objectives of this change are to protect biodiversity, prevent forest fires, combat climate change and improve the economic use of our forest resources. The conservation of the mountains is a key part of the protection of the biological diversity of these spaces that have been subjected to human influence since ancient times. The best way to conserve and protect the biodiversity they store is to manage them sustainably. Similarly, all experts state that sustainable forest management is the best barrier to protecting mountains from fires. In addition, the fight against forest fires and the sustainable management of our mountains are very important weapons in the fight against climate change. Sustainable forest management achieves adaptation and mitigation of the effects of climate change, being the sinking mountains of greenhouse gases. A sustainably managed stock is gradually adapting to climate change and can better combat desertification. On the other hand, the growth of the species that grow in our forest territory is almost twice the amount of wood that is used in it. This continued accumulation of biomass results in the danger of fire and increased severity and reduced forest productivity. In addition, it is the aim of this Government to boost the rural environment and ensure adequate livelihoods for its inhabitants. We cannot deprive them of the possibility of having new opportunities to defend and improve their interests. We must make sustainable use of the available resources that generate income, that generate jobs in the rural environment precisely for its inhabitants, that are the people who inhabit it and that care for it. This Government, ladies and gentlemen, particularly supports local development. The Plan for the Socio-economic Activation of the Forest Sector, adopted by the Government at the beginning of last year, made a diagnosis of the forest sector, which included the following aspects: the small size of forest ownership, the low management of mountains and the low level of associationism in our country. The result of this diagnosis is some of the amendments contained in this bill. I now turn to the most relevant aspects which this bill seeks to improve and which are the instruments necessary to achieve the objectives set. It is an objective, as I have pointed out, to improve the management of the Spanish mountains. To this end, first of all, its classification is changed, because the current law was confusing in its treatment. The new classification is based on two criteria: ownership, according to which mountains can be public or private, and the possibility of affection for a public service. In this way, separate and independent categories are established, so that a mountain can only belong to one of them. Public holdings are de manial and also perform a function of general interest. Private mountains that perform a function of general interest are protective mountains. The mountains which, being of public ownership, do not fulfil a function of general interest are heritage. And, finally, there are particular mountains whose ownership is private and does not fulfil a function of general interest. Ladies and gentlemen, with the existing legal obligation that all mountains with a surface area greater than that determined by each autonomous community have a management instrument, it is not taken into account that not all mountains are equal and have the same environmental value and do not perform the same functions; only their size is considered. The previous model, with the simple legal obligation, has not proved effective and the proportion of piles ordered remains low. The bill before you today imposes this legal obligation on public goods and all those who perform a function of general interest, which is given ten years after the entry into force of this law which is presented today to fulfil that obligation. At the same time, management instruments are simplified, facilitating and encouraging mountain holders to have a management instrument and to comply with it. It is therefore a question of replacing a model based on the enforcement obligation of the law with a system that encourages administratively that each holder has the most appropriate management instrument and that is most appropriate to him. The draft law provides for simpler and less forced forest management for those mountains that are considered by the autonomous communities. They will be able to establish management models adapted to the reality and typology of their mountains, to which the owners will be able to adhere and thereby assume the management commitment according to the model adopted by the autonomous communities. This model, effective and simpler, joins the other management models, which remain as they were established. In order to encourage management, the provision is maintained that ordered mountains only need to declare the use of wood to the forest organ in advance, without the need for authorization, except in the case where the use is among those to be subjected to environmental impact assessment. The bill, however, maintains the requirement of prior authorization for the use of unordered mountains. The exception in the case of short-shift farms, i.e. less than 20 years old, remains in force and small-scale farms, i.e. those with less than 10 cubic metres of wood, are added. Ladies and gentlemen, as a result of the successive de-amortisations that have affected our mountains, there are a million and a half hectares of mountains whose ownership belongs to several people, some of whom in many of these projects are unknown - these are the so-called partner mountains -. The additional provision provided for in the current law has made it possible to begin to manage some of these mountains. This bill provides a solution to all of them after the experience gained in these years and provides for a specific framework that will allow them to be managed without the need for agreement of all owners, given, as I say, that they are often unknown. To this end, and without prejudice to the civil procedures for the accreditation of the ownership of the identified non-proprietary parties, the vacant parties, a management board shall be established which shall include all known owners, and the presence of the four-fifths of them shall be sufficient for their validity. This management board shall be constituted as a community of assets, with the capacity to assume the acts of administration and management of the mountain, and shall notify the General Directorate of Heritage of the existence of vacant quotas. The General Directorate of Heritage will investigate its ownership and will finally decide whether to incorporate it into the General Administration of the State or increase the known quotas. The benefits from the management of the vacant parties will be invested in the improvement of the mountain. Forest companies are also regulated as capital companies or pooled management instruments, without loss of ownership of the area to promote mountain management and the generation of economies of scale. It is a group of owners of plots that can be used for forestry purposes, which assign the rights of forest use to the society created indefinitely or for a period of not less than 20 years, whose social purpose is the joint exploitation and exploitation of forest land. Its partners preserve all Sunday privileges, with the exception of forest management, given to society for a time in exchange for a share in their benefits. The aim is to avoid the impossibility of exploiting fragmented forest areas, as well as to put in management large forest areas now abandoned or semi-abandoned and to get their introduction into the productive circuit of the rural world. All this will allow the rational and sustainable exploitation of forest products in the mountains, and their protection will generate economic activity and social development, will contribute to creating new jobs and thereby curb the depopulation of the rural environment. This bill includes measures to combat illegal logging of timber, which is the main cause of deforestation on a global scale, and does so through the fight against its associated trade. In this sense, the draft law includes measures that allow the effective application in Spain of the European Union regulations on combating illegal logging with the utmost administrative simplicity for our forestry sector. The new law provides that public administrations shall cooperate in the field of their competence to ensure the legality of wood and its derived products introduced into the Spanish market and determines that agents opting for an individual system shall submit a responsible declaration to the competent autonomous body, which shall lay down the content of the declaration. To facilitate this work, the traceability of this system and the infringements and sanctions it entails, information registers are created that allow any autonomous administration to easily monitor forest products, both domestic and imported, and to trace the different steps they take through different autonomous communities or abroad. This makes it much easier to comply with the obligations imposed by the Community timber regulation. At the same time, the transmission of information to the European Union referred to in that Regulation shall be facilitated. The bill, in turn, provides for the introduction of the gravity of the damage caused as one of the factors, together with the delay in recovering the previous situation, which modulates the gravity of an offence, so that not only serious or very serious will be the offences that take a long time to recover, but also those that cause large-scale damage, even if recoverable in a relatively small time. Other aspects to be highlighted in the bill are as follows. Regulation of road traffic by forest runways. Traditionally, an unclosed runway has been considered transitable unless it is closed or the circulation is expressly prohibited. The 2006 amendment prohibited access to any runway that had not been visibly permitted. With the current change, the autonomous communities are left to choose one or the other model, with the obligation to allow access to fire-extinguishing vehicles, surveillance and management vehicles, and marking the obligation to regulate it, but above all maintaining the legal security of citizens above the model that each autonomous community determines. The 2006 Act provides for several exceptions to the impossibility of changing the use of forest land within 30 years of being affected by a fire. Nothing was required for this change of use and the possibility of the exception depended only on the time of administrative processing. Today's possibilities under the 2006 Act are supplemented by an additional one that requires that there be declared to be compelling reasons of first-class public interest in the burned area. In order to ensure full transparency of this new exception, compelling reasons of first-class public interest will be required to be declared by a law-class rule, which requires prior consultation with advisory bodies and broad public consultation. In addition, for these cases, a replacement of a surface equivalent to the one that has changed the use is required. The bill in turn seeks to promote forest plantations in abandoned agricultural land as an element in the fight against climate change. These plantations capture greenhouse gases, generate wood and are used - they can be used - on agricultural land that would otherwise be idle. Every cubic meter of wood that is thus obtained relieves the pressure on primary forests outside our borders, diminishing the ecological footprint of our industry, helping to combat climate change. In order to promote these plantations, which are usually short-cycle, soil characterization is eliminated as a mountain in these cases. The planting is subject to forestry legislation and when the production cycle is over the holder can choose from any forestry or agricultural crop, as the soil continues to maintain - it has not lost - this latter qualification. The aim is not to prevent with the change of irreversible use the use of agricultural land and pastures as forests temporarily with the aid of the CAP, the common agricultural policy. The functions of forest and environmental agents are systematised: they do not change their characteristics, their functions or their obligations and coordination with members of the security forces and bodies is improved when both participate in the same investigation, which occurs in approximately 2% of cases. The forest or environmental agent is responsible for identifying and interpreting evidence that may lead to the clarification of alleged criminal acts and the agent of the security forces and bodies is responsible for establishing the chain of custody of the evidence and conducting the investigation leading to it. These are the tasks that are currently marked by the legislation in force, so, as I have said, this draft does not bring anything new to the current situation. The status of agents of the authority of forest and environmental agents is maintained. Their duties and powers in police, administrative, forestry and environmental work, as well as the generic nature of judicial police, are also not changed with regard to the current situation. The new text provides for a way to ensure that coordination with the competent bodies in criminal investigations, which are the security forces and bodies, is rapid, fluid and reliable for the benefit of the investigation itself. Ladies and gentlemen, in closing my presentation, I would like to comment on the inclusion of measures for the management of kinegetics and fish stocks which the draft law envisages. There was a need for harmonization and coordination of important aspects for the proper exercise of these activities. To this end, in collaboration with the autonomous communities, the Ministry will develop a national strategy for kinegetic management as an orientation and coordination framework for the national management of kinegetic and fish exploitation, which will be approved at a sectoral conference and will contain aspects such as the regulation of hunting licenses, veto periods, kinegetic repopulations with indigenous species, the regulation of the hunting of migratory species, the regulation of the introduction of alien species, among others. The national register of hunting and fishing offenders is also established for the recognition of hunting and fishing licences among those communities that so decide. The existence of this register will facilitate the implementation of the mechanism of mutual recognition of hunting licences between autonomous communities, mutual recognition initially signed by four autonomous communities: Castilla-La Mancha, Castilla y León, Madrid and Extremadura, to which - to this, as I say, mutual recognition of hunting licences that will facilitate a licence to allow its hunters to hunt or fish throughout the territory of the autonomous communities - the autonomous communities of Asturias, Galicia, Aragon and the Valencian Community are joined. However, it is intended to promote healthy mountains that support our great biodiversity, as well as promoting the positive contribution to the global cycles of carbon and water, the protection of populations and infrastructure from natural disasters, as well as income generation and employment creation in rural areas, as well as the excellent conditions for providing recreation and cultural values for all. Thank you very much, President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u109	Thank you, Mrs. Jordà. Mrs. Ariztegui.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u110	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. Minister, you are insecure of discouragement. In other words, competitive and also clearly counterproductive interference. Let's go in parts. They do not mean any improvement in their functions or in good management of the territory and of course they mean an invasion of our sovereignty in every rule. We cannot admit that their functions remain effective and efficient, that they are being removed from the management and conservation of the environment that they have been developing for 150 years. It is the strong vision of a State that seeks to refocus competences, functions and powers through any of the initiatives within its reach. Similarly, the strengthening of the presence at sectoral conferences has another relevant consequence, which is manifested in the increased use of instruments for planning and managing public policies in a specific area, in this case relating to mountains, resources and forest exploitation, resulting in the State planning tools being used to control the matter in question, introducing uniform criteria for action with almost absolute disregard for the exclusive competences of the autonomous communities in this area, leaving these competences relegated to functions of mere implementation of those instruments. The attitude is rather perverse in this sense; a series of mechanisms are created so that it cannot be said that the autonomous communities are not competent; no, they are the ones who carry out the orders given from Madrid. In addition, with the aim of strengthening a centralist - I repeat - or state view of this matter, the Government has, as has been repeated, resorted to the role of market unity in another attempt to gradually regain autonomous powers: hunting, fishing and kinegetic species. To this end, it incorporates ad hoc instruments. You have incorporated a new instrument, which is the national strategy of kinegetic management and the Spanish registration of hunters and fishermen for that purpose. It has been said by other colleagues, and you know it perfectly well, that the subject of hunting is an exclusive competence of the autonomous communities, which are competent in this field. I don't know why they wanted to stick their finger in this story, maybe to please the hunters, etc. In this regard, the laws that each autonomous community has in this area are sufficient, clear and competent in all this. Besides, it's kind of a mattute, because they've put something into this law that might belong to you somewhere else. In any case, it is another incursion into a competent matter that is not yours. In the social environment, some of the contents of this project are preceded by an important response. This is, on the one hand, the provision which you make in paragraph 61 of the single article of this amendment of the law, which incorporates a new paragraph, by which the autonomous communities are empowered to - they say so - in the face of compelling reasons of first-class public interest, which must be assessed by law, to derogate from the prohibition of change of forest use, at least for thirty years, on burning forest land. Why do we all smell the same thing? It is not that we make an analogy with the issue of fires, but that we all smell the same with this modification. From different parties involved in this sector, a kind of incentive to burn forests is transmitted for the purpose of re-qualification of the corresponding land for urban purposes, which is the logical consequence that we all draw. Because, if not, what is the reason for this change? We don't understand. On the other hand, from the measures derived from the CORA, which are also used for this law, as other colleagues have commented, forest agents - whose representatives accompany us and whom I greet - are stripped of their character as public officials. This is very intentional and very premeditated, because they cease to be auxiliary to justice and become auxiliary personnel of the Security Forces and Corpses, so they can only act as administrative police in the face of facts that may constitute administrative misconduct. In addition, the functional reduction of these agents and the loss of their functional nature is perceived from the union world too, because we have received that complaint, as a masked formula, as other groups have cited, for a future privatization or possible privatization of this service. Finally, the text in question exempts certain types of mountains from the obligation to have instruments for the management of these areas directly linked to the territory and natural resources, for which article 45 of the Constitution obliges the public authorities to use them rationally in order to protect and improve the quality of life and to defend and restore the environment. What we have said in this speech is that, in the light of all these precedents, certain modifications are clear because the State is empowered to lay down basic rules on the conservation and sustainable use of forest genetic resources; in the area of hunting, the exclusive competence of the autonomous community is violated by this modification and, in the same sense, the State is entrusted with the management of the instruments necessary for the development of forest genetic resources. We believe that the allocation of powers to the State for the adoption of common basic guidelines for sustainable forest management infringes exclusive autonomous powers. We believe that the inclusion and exclusion of mountains in the catalogue of mountains of public utility should be done by agreement of the highest governing body of each autonomous community, thus violating their competence for the organization and allocation of their powers and functions. We believe that the creation of the National Register of Protective Mounts under the authority of the ministry concerned violates the autonomous regulations for the keeping of forest registers, and since judgment 71/1983 on the catalogue it has been established that these sectoral requirements are the property of the autonomous communities, as you well know. We also consider that Article 36(5), by assigning powers to an administration other than the holder of the mountain for the disposal of products or services from manial or public mountains, is not only contradictory to paragraph 1 of the same article, but is not aware of the matters in which the assets of the local entities holding the listed mountains are subject. We also believe that when you set for the State the minimum shift, for the purpose of taking advantage of short-shift spaces in twenty years, the competence of the autonomous communities to regulate this matter is unknown since they do not quote it, as it is a matter of exclusive competence of these communities. You, with the amendment of Article 61, create the National Register of Cooperatives, Enterprises and Forestry Industries. It should be raised under which constitutional protection this register is created, since it is established that sectoral registers on forest matter are also owned by the autonomous communities. Therefore, I believe that there is more than one invasion, more than one infringement, more than one ignorance that I do not consider to be involuntary, because you know that when you have amended this law, that it precisely modifies all those issues that I have cited and that all of them affect on the same flotation line: I award myself what was decided in its day that they were the competence of the autonomous communities and the Constitutional Court will then come to say that I am not right or, perhaps, to say that I am right, because we all know what the Constitutional judgments are like. We know what the configuration of this Parliament is and we will wait for the stage of partial amendments so that we can introduce those which we consider appropriate. Thank you very much, Madam President.

Instead of promoting public control and forest management instruments, they eliminate them. They have replaced prior administrative authorisation with a responsible declaration and, for cases where there was a management instrument, the prior notification regime is also replaced by a responsible declaration. Let's face it: it eliminates the ban on building in a burned area, opening the door to the urban classification of burned forests and, therefore, opening the door to the implementation of activities that have nothing to do with mountains, forest management or the protection of biodiversity. In 2006, the Montes Act introduced a ban on the change of forest use of burned areas for a minimum of 30 years, and now this project eliminates the ban, allowing for the change of forest use in a burned area, opening up the risk of fires for speculative purposes. The elimination of the ban on building in a burned area is a typical aberration of a government with a short-term vision that only watches over the interests of a few, It seems the last attempt to perpetuate economic development in a brick stroke, which runs counter to the common sense and the general interest of the citizens of this country. We do not have the justification that change has an objective horn to prevent arson from preventing actions of general interest. What we want is to avoid cases that, under the umbrella of the general interest, have already occurred in our country, such as that of Terra Mítica in Benidorm or the Ski Meseta in Tordesillas, with artificial snow and in a burnt ground, therefore, the general interest is not seen anywhere. The compelling reasons of public interest are no guarantee of anything. The two examples I have cited are perfectly clear. Spain has also been sentenced on three occasions by the Court of Justice of the European Union for failing to comply with this requirement. With regard to the reduction of elements of forest management and environmental guarantees, instead of promoting forest management instruments, they eliminate them by reducing environmental guarantees under a purely productive view of the mountains. Control over forest use is reduced, forgetting the conservation of biodiversity. They allow the approval of model forest management models, which makes it possible to dispense with the development of a specific planning instrument for each mountain or forest land. This could lead to the need not to apply for prior authorizations or communications to the environmental body, which would prevent in practice the monitoring of the uses and uses of the montage. - It is clear from paragraph 46, which amends Article 32.4-. They have abolished the obligation of management instruments for private non-protective and public uncategorized mountains, and in those cases where a forest management instrument is to be available, the time to have it is extended from fifteen to twenty-five years, further extending the period without forest planning. Not five or ten, twenty-five years, the equivalent of an entire generation. Is that space reasonable to have a forest management instrument? They eliminate the Natura 2000 Network from the assumptions to include it in the public mountains in the public utility mountains catalogue. They also remove the Natura 2000 network from the preference for incentives. The bill makes it possible for public mountains to host economic activities that have nothing to do with the production of goods and resources of a forest nature. In the opinion of this group, the conduct of service activities must be directly and necessarily linked to the protection of the environment, They have replaced the prior administrative authorization with a responsible declaration, in the woody and woody farms of short time, in the mountains not managed by the forest organ of the autonomous communities and in which there is no management instrument. They introduce an element of legal uncertainty in the performance of forest actors, which would become mere assistants to all the State Security Forces and Bodies, giving an incomplete and unrelated nature to the procedural legislation of the draft text. They make unequal and arbitrary treatment of forest agents in their capacity as judicial police in respect of bodies of a similar nature, such as the Customs Surveillance Service or local police, which are collaborating and not auxiliary - I insist, not auxiliary - to the State Security Forces and Bodies. They subordinate forest agents, thus leading to a loss of autonomy and efficiency, since the development of the same public function will require the participation of several public employees - forest agents, on the one hand, and State Security Forces and Bodies, on the other - where it is not currently necessary, thus complicating the actions. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u123	Thank you very much, Mrs. Lucio. Mr Barberà has the floor on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u124	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, this bill which has been approved by the People's Party Government amending Act 43/2003 of 21 November 2003 in Montes has received a number of refund amendments. From the Catalan Parliamentary Group of Convergence i Unió we have not tabled an amendment to the whole because some of the innovations introduced by this bill are adjusted and allow the implementation of the lines envisaged in the Convergence i Unió programme in the field of forestry and its sustainable development, and they are also aligned with the policies being implemented by the Government of the Generalitat. I'll highlight some of them. To begin with, I would like to highlight the novelty of considering mountains as green infrastructure as an inspiring principle of this law. We share that they constitute natural systems that provide first-class environmental services. The concept of multifunctionality of forests is also recognized, its ability to perform various economic, ecological and social functions simultaneously, with none to the detriment of the others. This idea is fundamental to us and to our position before this legislative initiative. We agree to consider forest land as a fundamental part of the rural world. It must contribute to its development, while serving as a key space for the maintenance of biodiversity. I would like to point out that from a social point of view it is not only important to have a playful service on these grounds; it is also necessary, essential, to reinforce the fundamental value they have in job creation, and this change makes it possible and allows it. With regard to the classification of forests, the dual criterion of ownership and allocation to a general interest is maintained, which is the factor that must mark the management of which they are to be subject. These affected forests maintain regulations and records that allow them to control with guarantees their special protection and safeguard. This bill continues to influence the need for planning as the best way to ensure sustainable forest management, but at the same time it is simplified by incorporating the possibility of adhering to a model type of forest management of each duly approved Autonomous Community and which can replace individual instruments, provided that the Autonomous Communities so permit. This measure will allow forest management through flexible instruments and, most importantly, at a lower cost to small farms. We also highlight the promotion of voluntary, transparent and non-discriminatory forest certification as a tool for sustainable forest management. In the field of forestry, the current system of authorisations and notifications is maintained, but the obligation to notify after use and only for statistical purposes is introduced. This initiative coincides with the improvement of the statistical field, which must be implemented by the autonomous governments, with the aim of allowing the improvement of the traceability of own products to ensure the legality of the origin of forest products. The initiative has an impact on compliance with existing European regulations in this area, by putting all means in place to prevent the introduction into the European market of timber and forest products of illegal origin. The national register of cooperatives, enterprises and forestry industries is established to serve as a tool for monitoring the traceability of forest products and also as a source of statistical information, a necessary basis for forest planning. With regard to mobility, access to mountains and the use of forest trails, which were previously generally prohibited, the decision is now left to each autonomous community, which must legislate on this issue. Perhaps here is the difference, in the sense of a competent invasion of this bill, because in Catalonia in this respect we already have our own legislation in this area. Therefore, since Convergence i Unió, we have no problem in that the State grants us more competences and responsibilities in this matter, as in another aspect, which other groups have criticized and which we assume with total responsibility, which is that it is the Generalitat of Catalonia that marks when a public interest can cause some burned land to have another use, that it can be urbanistic or it can be agricultural; it may not be urbanistic but it can be agricultural and have other functions and not be unproductive for so long. And I take this opportunity to say that, in the area of competition, our powers in the field of hunting and river fishing have been violated, and it will have to be corrected. If not, it will have to be the Constitutional Court that withdraws this competitive invasion and, as the Council of State's opinion says, it has been very good that with the previous law the Generalitat made different constitutional remedies. I continue with the positive aspects. Another aspect, in which we fully agree, is the necessary economic revival of the forest sector, in this case through the grouping for management purposes that is achieved by the introduction of forest societies, defined as the grouping of owners of parcels that can be used for forestry purposes that give the rights of forest use, indefinitely or for a certain period of time equal to or greater than twenty years, to the forest society. Partners maintain all property privileges except forest management, which is given to society for a period of time in exchange for profit sharing. The sectoral conference on agriculture and rural development and the National Forestry Council are established as bodies for the coordination and participation of forestry policy, the composition, organization and so on, which will have to be developed on a regulatory basis. These are the incorporated measures that we welcome, but this legislative initiative should have incorporated other measures necessary to further improve sustainable forest management, should be more ambitious, and in this regard we will present our partial amendments. The definition of forest land should have included facilities related to a first processing of wood that do not have significant added value, so that these activities do not need to be located on industrial land that can make them economically unviable. In farms without a management plan, the possibility of a communication regime should be left open in the case of exploitations with the declaration responsible for a duly qualified technician. It introduces the need for areas declared to be at high risk of fire to have a defence plan, with conditions that exceed the content of the prevention plans, as they deal with aspects of surveillance and extinction. We should leave this content as an orientation and not as defined in the current wording. With regard to forest agents, rural agents, with different names and functions in the rest of the State, we propose to delete articles that want to modify their functions, such as article 6.q. Subparagraph (q) aims to assign auxiliary functions to forest agents, rural agents. We believe that they should continue to act as hitherto, in a complementary and coordinated manner with the Security Forces and Bodies, a reason which you have justified and which is already being fulfilled. This initiative limits the capacity of forest and rural actors to act and research. We will also call for the deletion of the amendment to Article 58 because it limits the investigative capacity of forest agents, rural agents, to investigate environmental crimes and relegates them to act in an auxiliary manner. Ladies and gentlemen, we are proud of the work of our rural agents, the degree of coordination with other bodies of the administration and their dedication and effort in the conservation of the territory. I would like to take this opportunity to thank you for your presence and to greet your representatives. Minister, the mountains provide society with many kinds of environmental services. However, its holders or managers and the same mountain do not receive any economic return in return. It would be necessary to reflect on the implementation of measures to compensate for the environmental externalities provided by the mountains through their sustainable management. We believe that in order to promote the activities and investments that are dynamic in the sector and generate a positive perception of society, this legislative initiative should incorporate a basic measure that involves the implementation of a specific taxation for the forestry sector, to adapt the forest masses to climate change and to consolidate forests as the heart of bioeconomy within the framework of the European Union's sustainability objectives. Therefore, reading the various amendments as a whole, we see how they reflect very different ways of addressing the challenge of sustainable forest management. The Catalan Parliamentary Group of Convergence i Unió hopes that the bill will be supplemented during the course of the procedure with our partial amendments, which will propose measures that lack the government's initiative and that we believe are essential to bring about a qualitative leap over 64.2% of the area of Catalonia that is forested and that it should allow its ecological, economic and social potential to be developed, and to reconsider the changes that negatively affect rural agents, forested agents, and to leave it as it was working properly. Thank you very much.
