ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u129	Thank you very much, Mrs. De Lara. Draft law amending the system of protection for children and adolescents. [[File number 121/00131]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u130	Draft law amending the system of protection for children and adolescents. The Minister of Health, Social Services and Equality has the floor on behalf of the Government for the submission of the bill.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u131	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I am here this afternoon to present to the House the two bills with which the Government has proposed a comprehensive reform of the current legislation on the protection of children and adolescents, also naturally in response to the amendments to the whole of the Joint Group, the Members of the Republican Left of Catalonia, and the Catalan Parliamentary Group of Convergence and Union. It is good that we have this opportunity to exchange views and I hope that after seeing each other's positions we will come up with positions, especially on the issues that we now see differently, although I would also like to point out that we are starting from a very high level of consensus and that this is a law that has been very much worked out. The two groups that have amended all of them expressly recognize that they share many of the changes that are made in the draft laws and, as it could not otherwise be, their overall objective, which is nothing more than to improve the social, economic and legal protection of families and to respond to the current needs of children and adolescents living in our country. That consensus, as I said, has been emerging, has been working on it, because these two bills have been agreed between the various ministries and the autonomous communities. Of course, they have been informed by the Council of State, the Ombudsman and the General Council of the Judiciary and have also been held in collaboration with the main NGOs working with children and adolescents. It is also a modification of the child protection system in which many years have been working and, therefore, it is the result of the work of the ministry that I preside over, of course of the previous minister and also of the last stage of the socialist government. It is a working document and a very elaborate system in which all those who had something important to contribute have participated. This is a major reform affecting twelve very important laws: first, the Organic Law on the Legal Protection of Minors, which has been in force since 1996, but also the Organic Law on the Judiciary, the Comprehensive Law against Gender Violence, the Civil Prosecution Act, the Law on International Adoption or the Law on the Protection of Large Families. The reform sets up a system for the protection of children and adolescents in line with our current reality and will make Spain the first country to incorporate the best interests of the child in the terms established by the United Nations. In particular, it will strengthen care for children at risk of neglect, assistance with ill-treatment, and foster care and adoption, where procedures to provide a family environment for children have to be expedited. We understand that the effectiveness of the protection system guaranteeing these laws must reach children and adolescents alike from all over Spain, beyond the autonomous community in which they reside, the country in which they were born or the administrative situation in which they are located. The principles of this legislation are very clear: the priority - and this summarises very well what the spirit of the law is - of stable measures vis-à-vis the temporary ones, the relatives vis-à-vis the residential ones and the consensual ones vis-à-vis the imposed ones. The legislation I present fulfils the duty of the public authorities set out in article 39 of the Spanish Constitution, which reflects the obligation to protect the family and, in particular, minors; it must also comply with the principle of equality enshrined in article 14 of the Constitution, without discrimination on the basis of birth, race, sex, religion, opinion or any other personal or social circumstance. Therefore, the reform, as set out in the draft laws, aims to continue to ensure uniform protection throughout the territory of the State and to provide a reference for the autonomous communities in the development of their own legislation, while incorporating some of the new developments introduced by the legislative and regulatory activities of the autonomous communities themselves during these years. We do so, as is our obligation, in accordance with the international agreements that we have signed, in accordance with the recommendations of the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child, but also following proposals from other institutions such as the Ombudsman or the Senate Special Commission for the Study of the Problem of National Adoption and Other Related Issues, recommendations that have not been met so far. These are reasons for bringing this legislation forward, but the data are also very illustrative of the legislative need posed by the Government to the House. Of the 35,000 children under guardianship or custody of public administrations in 2013, 13,500 were waiting for a family. In 2013, 3,400 minors were victims of crimes against sexual freedom. We have recently known through the macro-survey of gender-based violence that 63 per cent of women who have suffered abuse, two thirds, say that their children witnessed, heard or were exposed to some situation of violence, and in many of these cases also the children themselves have suffered it. Therefore, every child, every adolescent in Spain is a sufficient reason for the reform that today brings us to the Congress of Deputies. This reform is based on three fundamental pillars: firstly, to establish a new framework for the rights and duties of minors, as well as greater protection for the particularly vulnerable; secondly, to regulate comprehensively the intervention of public administrations in situations of risk and neglect and to reform the measures of the protection system with special impact on reception and adoption; and thirdly, to prevent and combat violence in children as a cross-cutting axis. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, as I said, a new framework for the rights of minors is being established, with the best interests of the child as an interpretative principle, a law and a rule of procedure, as recommended by the United Nations in 2013, making Spain the first country to incorporate it, and with its right also to be heard in those procedures that affect them, which means that in the resolution of judicial proceedings it must be exposed if the child has been heard and what he has stated. Moreover, for the first time in national legislation, in line with other international and autonomous norms, minors are recognized as holders of duties in the family, school and social spheres, for example, to participate in family life, to have a positive attitude towards learning, to respect their teachers or to avoid school harassment. In addition, a new framework is established that affects foreign minors and recognizes them, under the same conditions as Spanish nationals and whatever their administrative situation, the right to education, the right to health care and social services, with particular regard to minors who are unaccompanied or trafficked. Children with disabilities are also guaranteed full accessibility and guarantee of specialized social services. For the first time since a standard, resources and support programmes are provided to facilitate the transition to independent life of young people aged 18 and over. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, minors are placed as genuine citizens with rights and duties, they are involved in the situations they live in and in their procedures and solutions. Ladies and gentlemen, secondly, the reform, as I said to you, constitutes a new system of child protection. Part of the definition of risk situations, for the first time also in a state norm, which comes to unify autonomous legislation and incorporate what we have learned through jurisprudence in recent years. As a result, a risk situation is considered in which, in certain circumstances, family or social deficiencies or conflicts, the personal, family and social development of the child is damaged. The risk will be declared by the corresponding public administration in accordance with both state and autonomous legislation, without it being possible to understand that there are invasions or competitive interferences, and in this case it will be mandatory to draw up a project of socio-family intervention that promotes and facilitates the protection of the child. From there, considering the risk, it is the public entities of the autonomous communities that can declare the helplessness, which will also be defined for the first time and which is determined by abandonment, the risk to life, health or physical integrity, as well as the induction to begging, crime or prostitution, never - I want to emphasize this because it is important - by the economic situation of the family. Once the existence of the helplessness has been declared, the guardianship of the child will be passed on to the corresponding entity, and here there is another important novelty because at that time only the prosecutor's office will be able to challenge it and not the biological parents. For the first time there is also a regulation on the transfer of children in helplessness from one autonomous community to another on a subject on which I believe, from the experience we have also had over the years, that it is necessary to increase coordination and to make the most of the effectiveness of public services. There is another alternative case in which it is the parents themselves who apply for voluntary custody of their minor children. For this reason, the reform establishes a maximum period of two years in order to prevent this custody situation from being chronicled, as has so far been the case in some cases. After these two years, the minor shall return to the family or a stable measure of protection shall be imposed on the family. In any case, the goal will always be the same: to keep the child with his or her family and, if that is not possible or is not convenient to the child's interest, to seek the best possible family. For this purpose, the formulas are family care and residential care and, as I said, because it is an inspiring principle of the law, the reform prioritizes family care over residential care, especially in the case of children under three years of age, where they will be sought to live with a family without going through a centre. It expedites this process by establishing that the prior intervention of the judge is no longer necessary and classifies it into three types according to the type of family and duration: firstly, emergency care, which is intended for children under six years of age, which is not longer than six months and is established until the most stable protection measure is decided; secondly, temporary care, with a maximum duration of two years, for when the minor is expected to be able to return to the family of origin or the minor is in an adoption process; thirdly, permanent care, after two years of the period, when the family reintegration of the minor is not possible. In addition, the reform includes - and this is important - a family-friendly status that includes the list of rights and duties. On the other hand, in the field of residential care, protection centres for children with behavioural problems are also regulated in the reform for the first time. It is in line with the reports published in recent years, including those of the Ombudsman. The rights of these children are clearly established, in what situation they may be derived from a protection centre and in what guarantees the centres have to offer. Each child will therefore have an individual protection plan to be reviewed every three to six months. Finally, the reform protection system incorporates some important developments in speeding up adoption to reach more children and more easily. In the case of national adoption, the criteria of suitability for adoptive families are unified, among which I would like to highlight the impossibility of adopting from those who are deprived of the parental power of a child. Procedures are also simplified, ladies and gentlemen. Custody is regulated for adoption purposes with the aim, as I explained to them earlier, of starting the cohabitation between the child and the appropriate family before the public body proposes the adoption to the judge. It facilitates the process for those who give their child up for adoption after birth. Open adoption is regulated, which allows the child in adoption to maintain some kind of contact with his or her biological family, provided that it is determined by a judge and with the approval of both the adoptee and the family of origin and the adoptive family. And the right to access to the origins of adoptees is strengthened, and public entities must ensure that information on that origin is kept for at least 50 years. In international adoption, the area of competence - I know that this is a question for some honourable Members - is clarified for the procedure of adoptions, so that the decision on a country and the accreditation of the collaborating entities will become the responsibility of the State. This is because it affects foreign policy - as you know, the Constitution in its article 149.1.3.a confers on the State exclusive competence in the field of foreign relations - and, as the draft laws also point out, it is done without prejudice to the necessary intervention of the public entities of the autonomous communities, which of course develop - and the law recognizes it at all times and I highlight it from this rostrum - a fundamental task. In all these cases, the draft laws provide for the participation, at times binding, of the Autonomous Communities, on the one hand, in determining the initiation of the adoption process with each country of origin of the minors - and also in the case of suspension or cessation - and, on the other, in the accreditation of the collaborating entities, where a prior report is required of the public entity in whose territory the body is based and the competent public entity is given its control, inspection and monitoring with regard to the activities to be carried out in the territory of that autonomous community. I believe that this guarantees the essential participation of the Autonomous Communities in this area. I would also like to refer to a mention made by the groups that have tabled an amendment to the whole of the economic impact, which may also be of concern to other groups, of this new system of protection. First of all, I want to remind you that what is important here is the best interests of the child and that, as the new legislation wants, the child with whom he is best is with his family - I think we all agree on that - and when he cannot be with his family the important thing is that he can have a new family. That's what the law wants. But, in addition, family care, which is the one that has been encouraged since the reform, if we talk about money, has an average cost of between 20 and 30 euros per day, while residential care amounts to more than double, 60 or 80 euros. It is more expensive and, of course, much worse for the child's interest. Let us not therefore look at this reform from an economic impact point of view. Ladies and gentlemen, thirdly, the reform strengthens the instruments of the public authorities in preventing and combating violence in children. For the first time, minors are recognized as victims of gender-based violence by amending the 2004 Act. [[Applauds]] In addition, the obligation of judges to rule on precautionary measures affecting the children of abused women is laid down and it is envisaged that these children will be able to remain with their mothers. The child's helplessness may be determined when there is a situation of ill-treatment, sexual abuse or emotional abuse. It establishes the obligation to bring cases of sexual abuse to the attention of the Public Prosecutor's Office for any citizen who knows it and constitutes an indispensable condition for access to professional activities involving contact with children on a regular basis not having been convicted of crimes against sexual freedom, trafficking or exploitation of minors. We will also take advantage of the procedure to incorporate some of the provisions of the Lanzarote Convention concerning the registration of pederastas. Finally, I would also like to refer to other important developments that present this reform, on the one hand, the obligation that the memories - this is also important - of impact analysis of pre-draft laws and draft regulations include the impact on childhood and adolescence. It is not simply sectoral legislation, but all legislation, legislation emanating from the Government or from this House, has an impact and is therefore intended to be cross-cutting and can be reported on. On the other hand, a shared information system is created between the Autonomous Communities and the General Administration of the State to advance a global understanding of the situation of the protection of children and adolescents, an instrument that we need and that will undoubtedly lead to an improvement in the design and evaluation of public policies affecting them. I believe that this also has to be a constant in every rule that we push, the improvement of information and knowledge, and above all the evaluation of public policies and the laws that we push from this Parliament. Finally, as you know, the Law on the Protection of Large Families is also amended with a very simple aim, which is that large families do not lose the title and status of large family until the last of the children has reached the established age, which is twenty-one years or twenty-six years if they are studying. This is a change that has been very much demanded by associations of large families and which also anticipates the comprehensive plan of support for the family in which the ministry that I am honoured to preside over is working and which I hope that in the coming weeks we will be able to take to the Council of Ministers and present also to your ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen, we are addressing a comprehensive reform that gives an effective response to the social changes that have taken place in Spain in recent years, which complies with both the Spanish Constitution and international standards and, in particular, follows the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child. It is a reform that strengthens the Government's commitment to the family, in particular to children. We are therefore acting and acting decisively so that this legislation in which we have been working for so long can see the light. I will listen with all interest to the groups, both those who have tabled amendments in their entirety and those who have not, and who generally share the forecasts that this amendment proposes. If I want to anticipate my absolute predisposition to dialogue and consensus. Many times when we meet with organizations dedicated to the protection of children, the protection of the rights of minors, children and adolescents in Spain, they always tell us: why don't you promote a pact for children? This is very important legislation. I think we all show them our willingness to do so. I understand that there are some difficulties, but those difficulties are not insurmountable. I also understand that there is a capacity to make proposals and to make improvements in the texts proposed by the Government, which, as I say, have been very much worked out, but, frankly, if in any of the aspects that are important for Spanish life and society we have to agree, it is well worth doing for Spanish children and adolescents. All the previous work has been done to make this happen. I will put on my side everything I have to put on and I expect from you the same disposition. Thank you very much, Mr President. [[Applauds]]

Even in the explanatory statement there is a point of adanism. In Catalonia there has been legislation on the protection of children since 1991 - since 1991 legislation is under way in Catalonia - and it culminated in 2010, within the framework of the codification of civil law in Catalonia, in a true code on the protection of children, in 2010, very recently; a global code, ambitious, modern, which incorporates, I insist, much of the philosophy of this bill. On the other hand, when one reads the explanatory statement, it seems that the one who has discovered the Mediterranean is the current government. Minister, the first question to be resolved is to clarify, as was the 1996 Act, the supplementary nature of State legislation. We must remember - Mr Tardà has said it and it is very clear - two articles of the Statute of Autonomy of Catalonia. One of them is 166, which establishes exclusive competence in the field of child protection, which is very clear: the regulation of the protection regime and of public institutions for the protection and protection of children at risk, as well as the regulation and management of public and private entities, services and establishments providing services in their territorial area. There is another very relevant competence, which is that contained in 129, which establishes exclusive competence in the field of civil law in its development. I'll say something about that later. We do not have time to make here a reminder of the origins of civil law and the fact that private civil law in Catalonia persisted in 1914, 1939 and 1978, but what is very clear is that Spanish civil law applies extraordinarily to Catalan civil law. In this there is a very broad development of the Constitutional Court, and even in the unfortunate judgment of the Statute of Autonomy of 2006 the thesis is maintained that what the Parliament of Catalonia can do is to modernize those institutions that make up part of that civil law, which is what we have done since 1980 in the Parliament of Catalonia. This allows me, Minister, to address what is most brutal about this bill, which is the liquidation of the possibility that, at least in Catalonia, we can have our own powers in international adoption, which we have exercised so far. Here I would like to remind you - Mr Tardà has mentioned this to you in the past but I want to stop for a moment - that in 1960, in the midst of Francoism, the compilation of civil law in Catalonia regulated the institution of adoption. Logically, with the recovery of Catalonia's capacity for self-government, the Catalan Parliament, developing this figure and adapting to a new reality such as the possibility of adopting a child outside the borders of Spain, regulated international adoption on the basis of the competitive title of civil law. This has never been the subject of political or judicial controversy. In the development of a competition such as that of civil law, the Government of the Generalitat - also the whole of the autonomous governments - has been allowed to determine with which countries the families of Catalonia can initiate the adoption procedure and through which non-profit entities this possibility is exercised. It is the Government of Catalonia that decides with which countries and through which entities. That has worked reasonably, so reasonably that you yourselves in the discussions we have had these years have said that Catalonia has exercised these policies well. Well, in your bill, this package of issues that the autonomous communities have been developing so far goes on to be developed directly by the General Administration of the State through the corresponding ministries and reduces the active role that Catalonia has played in this matter to almost a simple negotiation of the ministry that you run. With regard to international adoption, my parliamentary group is particularly proud that a colleague of ours, Conchita Tarruella, from 1994 to 1998, served as Director-General of Child Welfare and much of the development of the Catalan model that we are defending today was the result of Mrs Tarruella's work. Some of the first countries in which families were able to initiate adoption proceedings were promoted by the Government of the Generalitat in Mrs. Tarruella's time. The only autonomous community that has created an autonomous body dedicated to this issue is the Catalan one through the Institut Català de l'Acolliment, which, moreover, through the Hague Convention, has recognized the role of competent authority and, therefore, of authority capable of carrying out such actions. The same agreement recognizes the role of the General Administration of the State as central authority and to provide information in communications between the Catalan Administration and the Government of the country of office. Moreover, those families that choose to process adoption not through the specialized entity, but directly through them - what is known as the public protocol - the relations are established between the Government of the Generalitat and the Government of that country directly. That worked. It is not that we are claiming it, it is not that we are claiming a new competition; we are claiming that something that has been peaceful and that has functioned reasonably remains in force. My group is shocked, Minister, and we are convinced - I take note of your attitude - that this has a solution, so far. More than 5,000 boys and girls have been adopted since 2007 to date in Catalonia, one third of all adoptions in the Spanish State. Some colleague, some colleague, Mr Guillaumes, is part of that group of adoptive families who have found in that relationship with the Generalitat, also through the specialized agencies, the proximity of the accompaniment that has allowed things to work reasonably. Why should what has so far worked properly be changed? The last reason for our amendment is that the tenth final provision incorporates a mandate not to increase expenditure. It seems to us that in childhood policy, Spain spends much less, invests much less than the whole of the European Union and that assertion contained in the draft law is a renunciation of having a policy of caring for children, especially vulnerable children, in line with the States of our environment. Our spending on the family, our spending on children should shame us; therefore, to give up increasing investment in children is to give up investing in the future. This also leads us, Minister, to reject this bill and to hope that this attitude which you have shown during the first part of your speech will move from words to facts and we will be able to find solutions. We will be open to this, but today we will call for the rejection of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u137	Thank you, Mr. President. Generally speaking, it follows the view of the International Treaties and Agreements Act and should therefore be interpreted in accordance with that agreement of the Bilateral Commission on the Foreign Action Act. As you well know, in that we disagree. If the autonomous public entities are reserved the functions of collaboration in the law throughout the procedure; they even participate in the determination of foreign policy aspects in this area, although it is de facto the General Administration of the State that determines the initiation of the adoption process in each country of origin, its cessation or suspension. It could be said that the formal and main decisions are taken by the General Administration of the State, although it is true - I will not deny it - that the text appears from the hand of the autonomous public entities; therefore, they themselves can directly exercise brokering functions in the country of origin. ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u140	Thank you, Mrs. Díez. For the Plural Left Group, I have the floor, Mr Pérez Moya, and I thank him for his willingness to speak so soon in plenary.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u141	Thank you, Mr. President. Minister, from La Izquierda Plural we understand that a great opportunity is lost to be able to develop a law for the protection of children and adolescents in an integral way, or what we could define, in its maximum splendour, a law on children and adolescents where all legislative bodies can be harmonized, including the Spanish Constitution of 1978, which does not fully recognize - although it does mention it - the participation in Spanish society of children and adolescents in our country in a democratic way and as full citizen actors. Similarly, it is not in its entirety a law on the protection of children and adolescents that recognizes the legislative changes that affect the professional cadres - social workers, judges, prosecutors, various administrative authorities, doctors, teachers, etc. - that are going to work with children and adolescents rather than the rights of children and adolescents themselves, as well as their facilitation for their demand to these professionals and institutions that are going to work with, for and for them. This law provided an opportunity to address the challenges posed to us by the reality of childhood and adolescence and to bring our legislation into line with the international standards of rights and responsibilities of children and adolescents expressed and recognized in the Convention on the Rights of the Child, adopted by the United Nations General Assembly. The misadjustment and inadequacies of our legislation have been identified in the concerns and recommendations repeatedly made to Spain by the Committee on the Rights of the Child, which underline the inconsistency of the laws and regulations applied in the autonomous communities with the Convention on the Rights of the Child in important areas such as the protection of children at risk or under family care, or in the treatment accorded to unaccompanied foreign children. This project only partially captures the observations made by the United Nations and, above all, loses the opportunity to conceive of children with their own identity as subjects with recognition of rights and responsibilities, ensuring that children grow up in a safe environment and with the necessary support. The Convention on the Rights of the Child recognizes children as capable persons, with certain rights and duties, with the right to express their views, decide and do, to a greater or lesser extent, in all matters affecting them. The convention does make explicit and clear transit, from considering children as the object of protection to a conception of children as social subjects of rights reflected in three basic principles: provision, protection and participation of children. We lack the coherence of the law in this approach. All of this leads us to state clearly that this law is insufficient. A genuine body of legislation on childhood and adolescence needs to be set up, in line with the Union, and not just make small improvements in legislative regulation. It is very important to address the full spectrum of risk factors that threaten the lives of children, adolescents and their families, emphasizing the dimension of cross-cutting participation in everything that affects them. The law continues to be discriminatory through the language used. The whole text uses the concept of minors, minors or minors from a non-inclusive point of view as full citizens of the law, including chapter III on the duties of minors. Thus, the balance of the law is lost in the protection of children and adolescents, as they are treated from a discouraging perspective as social actors. When we talk about discrimination through the language used, we refer to the lexicon used correctly in the rights approach proclaimed by the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child. This leads us to ask ourselves again about the protectionist purpose of this law. We note that, at present, the use of the term minor has been relegated to punitive legislation such as penal codes or various international treaties. On the other hand, we must point out that, as a text that attempts to harmonize existing international treaties with the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child, we are deeply shocked by its insistence throughout the text not to accommodate the correctly used terms when addressing children and adolescents in favour of the term minor, which is no longer used in international legislation. With regard to the use of gender in the body of the law, children and adolescents also appear as an asexual category; there is neither a trace of inclusive language nor mention of the diversity of situations according to gender, nor of other characteristics or personal conditions, which causes an objective and subjective disregard of the assumptions analyzed by the law in view of the future of their application in real life. It is important to note that the law refers to the best interests of the child rather than the best interests of the child. This leads to leaving participation in the processes that really affect children and adolescents on the side of the whole law, especially leaving children and adolescents in our country completely unaware of their rights. They cannot participate strictly in any social and judicial circumstances affecting them in which they are seen as direct and indirect protagonists. See the example of the right to be heard in adoption proceedings, while the Convention on the Rights of the Child requires the right to be heard, as recommended by UN General Comment No. 10 on the Convention on the Rights of the Child. The difference is substantial and it is not vague because being heard in the judicial sphere is not the same as being heard. It consecrates a vision of childhood and adolescence as an object to be protected, since it is in the best interest of others to decide. Therefore, they are not considered as social actors or citizens of our society, but they are still subjects or objects to care for and protect, far from facilitating an evolution in an individual society of growth and not of controlled maturation. There are other considerations we would like to take into account. One would be the public entity as the managing body. The emergence of this body in the legislation analysed raises serious doubts about the subrogation of the functions of previous bodies in operation for adoptions, receptions, etc., which could lead to new cases of privatization of the treatment of the law of the person and its serious consequences, which would lead to a commercialization to the detriment of legal certainty in the field of children and adolescents in our country. Are we faced with a new US court figure for the management of these processes with children and adolescents of a private and profit-making nature? How will this new figure and its relationship with the autonomous communities and their legislation be regulated? In relation to the competition issue, the reaction of the law implies a very clear invasion in those communities which have recognized in their statutes of autonomy exclusive competence in the field of the protection of minors, and reference has already been made to this issue by previous speakers. In the case of Catalonia, article 166 of its statute of autonomy spells out the exclusive competence of the Generalitat with regard to the protection of minors, which in any case includes the regulation of the regime for the protection of public institutions for the protection and protection of minors at risk and of juvenile offenders, in the latter case respecting the criminal legislation. In addition, Catalonia has a law on rights and opportunities in children and adolescents, which regulates subjects that fall within its competence. The amendment proposed in Organic Law 1/196 of 15 January, on the legal protection of minors, on the partial amendment of the Civil Code and the Civil Prosecution Act, ignores the reality of autonomous communities with their own civil law such as Catalonia. Catalonia holds exclusive jurisdiction over civil law, in accordance with Article 129 of its statute of autonomy, with the exceptions provided for in Article 149.1.8a of the Spanish Constitution. Therefore, the amended articles of the Civil Code in the present draft law on child protection should be applied as an alternative in those autonomous communities with jurisdiction over civil, foral or special law. Finally - and I conclude - it is clear that this bill entails an increase in expenditure in terms of staffing, salaries and staff costs. This contrasts with the evolution of allocations from the State General Budgets to care for children and families, which have been halved since 2011. These and other considerations and proposals that I cannot develop due to lack of time will be raised in the partial amendments. So far it would be the explanation of the position of the Plural Left. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

[[Applauds]] That is social cuts and not what this government has done, which has been betting on childhood and on the family! Let us therefore stop speaking about electoral campaigns and really take advantage of the opportunity offered by these two bills. Having said that, I also thank the Socialist Parliamentary Group for announcing collaboration with the Government. Of course, the People's Parliamentary Group thanks him very much because there will be very close collaboration over these days until its final adoption. But do not regret the delay in the introduction of the bill, when for seven years of socialist government in this House we have not discussed how we are doing this afternoon after three years of People's Party government. [[Applauds]] In any case, my thanks for the will you have shown. With regard to the amendments to the whole that had been tabled, it was said that it represented a competitive invasion in those communities that had recognized exclusive competence in the protection of minors in their autonomy statutes. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to point out that the main purpose of the draft law is to ensure, precisely, the social, economic and legal protection of the family, and in particular that of minors, in accordance with international agreements and with the provisions of the Constitution in its article 39, as well as to ensure that the modification of the system of protection for children and adolescents ensures compliance with the principle of equality enshrined in the Spanish Constitution, which allows children to continue to be guaranteed uniform protection throughout the Spanish territory, and that it constitutes a reference for the autonomous communities in the development of existing legislation. Ladies and gentlemen, the exclusive competence of the Statutes for the protection of minors has as limits - and we should all know this - the powers which are exclusive to the State, including those relating to civil law. As for the fact that they say that the reality of the communities with regard to their own civil law in Catalonia is ignored in this project, in no case does this group share it, since the recognition of exclusive competence of the autonomous communities in the field of civil law by their statutes does not upset the competence of the State, as provided for in article 149 of the Constitution, and as regards the amendment of the Law on International Adoption and constitutes a flagrant invasion of autonomous competence, nothing further from reality. Ladies and gentlemen, the decision to authorise adoptions in the different countries of origin, as well as the function of accrediting collaborating entities for adoption, is based on Article 149.1.3a of the Spanish Constitution, which confers on the State exclusive competence in the field of international relations, and there is also a ruling of the Constitutional Court, which includes within the competence of foreign relations, to reserve the State the possibility of establishing measures regulating and coordinating the activities with external projection of the autonomous communities, in order to avoid or remedy any damage to the direction and implementation of foreign policy which is exclusively the responsibility of the State authorities. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law establishes channels for the participation of the autonomous communities, not as has been said this afternoon, since, although the State is given the power to authorize adoptions in the different countries of origin, as well as the role of accrediting the collaborating entities for international adoption, in both cases the participation of the autonomous communities is envisaged. In conclusion, the law is respectful of the constitutional and statutory division between the State and the autonomous communities, where the best interests of the child prevail. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, the General Council of the Judiciary, the Fiscal Council and the General Council of the State support the regulation provided for in this draft in those matters which have been the subject of the amendments as a whole. As far as economic viability is concerned, the economic impossibility that you argued, well, I would like to point out two things: on the one hand, the new measures envisaged in the draft law will lead to an improvement in the child protection system and, on the other hand, some of the measures envisaged will allow a better use of the budget headings for the care of minors, such as the application in this case of family-friendly measures with regard to residential care, as the Minister has explained. And let us not forget that the governments of the People's Party, the General Administration of the State are always governed by the principles of efficiency and efficiency, both administrative and budgetary, and that is a guarantee of the sustainability of the system. Ladies and gentlemen, going back to what we are dealing with, to the draft law, I would like to highlight - the Minister has already done so - in the short time that I have some relevant points left. First, the rule establishes a new framework for the rights and duties of minors, with special attention in this case to the most vulnerable, such as unaccompanied foreign minors, extolled young people, children with disabilities, etc. The bill promotes the participation of children through the right to be heard and heard. The reform for the first time includes national legislation and a catalogue of duties in the family, school and social spheres. Secondly, the reform constitutes a new system of child protection, and it is important to stress that the draft clearly states that the economic situation of the family is never a reason to declare the situation of helplessness, since one of the objects of the rule is regular in this regard. As for voluntary custody, it is limited to two years in order to prevent these situations from becoming chronic, something that was happening. At the same time, foster care and adoption procedures are speeding up, with the reform giving priority to family solutions compared to residential, state and temporary solutions, and consensual ones compared to those imposed. The aim is for at least all children under three years of age to live with a family without passing through a reception centre. Ladies and gentlemen, the minister has explained, quite frankly, what are the reception formulas and I am not going to detail any more. What does seem important to me as a novelty is that the reform points out, before the public body proposes to the judge the constitution of the adoption, that a period of cohabitation between the child and the family considered appropriate can begin, so that the children always remain in a family and are thus prevented from passing through a reception centre. Another notable development is open adoption. Of course, ladies and gentlemen, one of the most important facts for me is that the processes of international adoption will be given greater legal certainty, as the area of competition will be clarified. Finally, the minister said, the fight against violence in children refers in this case to the recognition of minors as victims of gender-based violence. Ladies and gentlemen, boys and girls can't wait. It is much that this reform, which has been demanded for a long time, is ambitious and we must not delay it. In their amendment they stated that any measure that improves the protection of minors is always welcome. Well, this project is. Withdraw the amendment in its entirety and put the defence of the best interests of the child before its absolutely nightly nationalist postulates. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u148	President, just one question because I do not know whether we have understood correctly. Is there a debate on a first draft law, even though the Minister has dealt with the two drafts in his speech, we finish that debate and start the next one?
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u149	That will be the case, and indeed the times in each debate are the established ones. Each of the interveners will have them. But I insist that whoever can make an effort of conciseness I, on behalf of everyone, will thank him.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u150	Mr President, I understand that these are two very important bills and two amendments to the whole which are also very important and which we want to defend with conciseness given all the work that the Socialist Group has done.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u151	I have no doubt that none of the speakers will cease to express all their arguments, as is their obligation. Thank you. I give the floor to the Minister of Justice.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u152	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, as my speech has shown, it is in the presentation of the two draft amendments to the Civil Procedure Act and to the Organic Law on the Judiciary that the Government has referred to these Chambers in a context in which there is a certain difference between evolution and revolution, between incorporating successive improvements and constant or sudden and supposedly definitive reversals, which has much to do with the level of prudence and risk that we assume when we carry out these types of projects. If it is true that the reformist strategy compensates for its lack of spectacularity with a constant pace of progress, history also shows that the bet for a whole or nothing is prodigy in failures. This typology helps us understand why these two bills modernise certain aspects of the administration of justice by following the reformist strategy clearly, even though the groups have been able to miss a global transformation that some suddenly yearn for. In other words, while the Government has wanted to change different things that it attaches importance to in itself, some do not identify with the technique and demand a single reform even if we do not know very well what that reform consists of. [[The lady]] The reform of the Civil Prosecution Act, as well as that of the Organic Law of the Judiciary, is part of a strong initiative to modernise Spanish justice, but it does so without the need to alter much of the procedural and organizational structures in which we must continue to be supported. This is therefore a package of measures incorporating a number of objective progress which, as such, must be assessed without an amendment to the whole system, as is done here in some of the proposals presented here on the projects to be discussed. Maintaining the most recognizable features of the model prevents us from making very significant improvements to it, asking legal operators and justice professionals to make a reasonable effort to change and ultimately to evolve and progress. These are the objectives of the reform, although it must be established that many of these objectives citizens have been waiting for time; waiting for us to be able to incorporate the new information society into the administration of justice. That is why the incorporation of new technologies is one of the objectives of the reform of the Civil Prosecution Act. Please note, ladies and gentlemen, that by 2020 what is called digital natives in our country will be 65% of the population and by 2025, almost 85%. The contact of citizens and professionals with digital reality has so far been far superior to the technical means at their disposal for the performance of their duties by civil servants and judicial professionals. The other three objectives of the reform of the Civil Prosecution Act are the improvement of the verbal judgment, a greater defence of consumers against unfair clauses and the updating of the statute of limitations for personal actions in the Civil Code. These three are, therefore, the fundamental objectives to which I wish to refer. First, there is the possibility that prosecutors will also be professional collaborators with the justice system. It is well known, because I believe it has been shown by the media, that from 1 January 2016 professionals and judicial bodies will be obliged to use the existing telematic systems in the Administration of Justice for the presentation of writings and documents and for the conduct of procedural communication acts. The law will allow the citizen to be notified with full effect via e-mail or mobile devices if he has chosen to do so. Acts of communication shall be carried out on a compulsory basis through electronic means for legal persons by means of what is called the single electronic address. In addition, SMS-type notices may be sent indicating that there is a notification. The formal requirements for communication acts will also be simplified, and it is no longer necessary for the prosecutor to always be accompanied by two witnesses. We are accused in one of the amendments of not having had sufficient means to make the transmission of the writings by telematics a reality. In my opinion, ladies and gentlemen, nothing less true since the necessary work is already being carried out, although it should be remembered that this is a shared responsibility for various public administrations and, therefore, we hope that the computer systems - the Lexnet platform, which is already ready for use in the various civil, social and contentious jurisdictional orders - will be an effective reality from January 2016. The only thing that is required for their use by those groups that do not yet employ them is for them to be discharged in the census, for the body representing them to prepare the necessary web service and minimum training. The second objective I mentioned to you is to improve the regulation of the oral trial. The written answer to the request is introduced to give greater certainty to this process, since the plaintiff will go to trial knowing the grounds for opposition of the defendant, so that both parties can be prepared and with the necessary means of proof. The summons of unnecessary witnesses will also be avoided and expert judicial evidence made feasible without the need to suspend the hearing, as is currently the case. This amendment is the result of the experience of fifteen years, since the adoption of the reform of the Civil Prosecution Act and, in this regard, of the demands we have received from various legal operators. Ultimately, it is a matter of strengthening the guarantees deriving from the constitutional right to effective judicial protection, avoiding the defencelessness of the parties and allowing the equality of arms without the need for delays in processing. It is precisely the opposite of what some of the groups have wanted to see when, for example, they have not understood that the failure to hold hearings when the parties so request and the judge considers it appropriate, rather than harming the citizen what it does is to offer him more advantages, such as the reduction of judicial response time or the saving of procedural costs, always maintaining all his rights, because it will only apply if the dispute revolves around purely legal issues or there is no need to make evidence different from the documentary evidence. Thirdly, with the reform now introduced in the monitoring proceedings and in accordance with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 14 June 2012, the judge, when referring to contracts concluded between consumers or users, will necessarily assess the existence or otherwise of these unfair clauses and, if there are such clauses, may, if there are, automatically dismiss the claim for payment. In addition, such a clause may not be invoked in any subsequent trial. Thus, far from ambiguities, a clear and precise procedure is envisaged for the judge to verify ex officio the existence or otherwise of such clauses in contracts, in all cases respecting the principle of contradiction, hearing and proximity of the parties, which is absolutely necessary if effective judicial protection is to be ensured. Fourthly and lastly, in accordance with the measures taken by the Government to provide families and individuals with a second opportunity, the Civil Code will be amended to allow personal actions to be prescribed at the age of five against the current fifteen. The interests of the subjects involved in the prescription are balanced. The time limit shall not be interrupted if, within one year from the extrajudicial claim, the debtor had failed to comply and the creditor had failed to claim judicial compliance. This ensures that successive out-of-court claims can delay the statutory limitation period. Contrary to what has been said in some amendments, ladies and gentlemen, I can assure you that this reform is being carried out on the basis of the work carried out by the General Committee on Codification and therefore with deep reflection. These are, to a large extent, improvements made by a project whose purpose and approach must be sought in its own content, in its own objective contribution and in its reforms rather than in declaratory principles. In any case, through the work of your ladies and gentlemen, the groups and the amendments, we will be able to improve on these concrete, timely measures that speed up and improve our civil justice. As far as the Organic Law of the Judiciary is concerned, you will agree with me, ladies and gentlemen, that one of the fundamental objectives of any reform in the field of justice must be its acceleration, the improvement of the times of response. There are, therefore, a number of measures in the draft reform of the Organic Law on the Judiciary that are clearly oriented in this direction, for example, the more homogeneous distribution of workloads between different courts and tribunals. To this end, the governing bodies may agree on amendments to the rules for the distribution of the courts of the Mercantile, Minors, Prison Surveillance, the Dispute-Administrative or the Social, in order to ensure the balance of the workload between all of them. On the other hand, it will encourage the care of victims of gender-based violence, victims of violence against women, by the specialized bodies we have set up, in such a way that the jurisdiction of the courts of violence can be extended to more than one judicial party, which will serve, in addition to providing better care to the victims, to discharge work to the courts of first instance and instruction or to the courts of instruction that have these powers at this time. The second possibility of improvement in which we propose to work has to do with the specialization of judges and magistrates as well as with the design of the courts. The bill provides for the specialization of one or more judicial bodies, on a temporary and exclusive basis, if determined on the basis of the accumulation of cases, in order to prosecute and resolve certain specific cases and so that they may have a provincial scope. It will facilitate the unification of criteria. It is an improvement that would certainly have been appreciated by those who have had to go to court in matters such as preferential actions, large employment regulation files or unfair clauses. This rule excludes only that matters relating to matters already assigned to bodies of different classes as well as to the courts of instruction can be attributed through this procedure, in order to avoid interference between the jurisdictional systems. Similarly, judges may be seconded to the investigating body for support work in complex cases; they will be useful in matters related, for example, to tax offences, urban issues or trade issues that are undoubtedly of high complexity. In the same vein, it is proposed to encourage the specialization of judges and magistrates. To this end, experience will be evaluated in specific cases and evidence will be convened to confirm their level of specialization, so that they can be recognized as specialists. As far as specialization is concerned, ladies and gentlemen, it is set out in Article 98. More flexibility and better coverage are now simply introduced, as these mechanisms can already be implemented through distribution systems. Similarly, the exemption of cases was already provided for a particular court for distribution, freeing the most overburdened. Therefore, what we do is to empower already existing instruments. The third improvement introduced by this draft reform of the Organic Law of the Judiciary seeks to increase the quality of judicial decisions and to give greater coherence to some fundamental aspects of our justice, to begin with, by reducing the uncertainty that arises as a result of judicial decisions sometimes of different meaning in cases or in similar circumstances. In order to compensate for this, non-jurisdictional plenary sessions are established, aimed at the unification of criteria, in order to avoid conflicting decisions between sections of the same judicial body. On the other hand, the direct civil liability of judges and magistrates is eliminated by assimilating it with that of other public employees and by complying with the recommendations of the Council of Europe in this area. Finally, in a very relevant way, the draft proposes the reform of the administrative and contentious cassation, admitting the appeal for cassation for reasons other than merely economic ones and extending it to matters of relevance to the Supreme Court, so that, regardless of the amount of the case, there is no cause that can be exempted from its control. Fourthly, ladies and gentlemen, the project aims to promote the professionalisation of the bodies serving the administration of justice. In this singular way it attends to and reinforces the role of the judicial secretaries, now called lawyers of the Administration of Justice, which, although some amendment does not value it this way, corresponds to a historical claim of this collective. This change does not diminish its functions in the direction of the judicial office, which is expressly mentioned, and even less so the principles of neutrality and impartiality which inform all its proceedings, on the contrary, add new powers, such as mediation, processing and, where appropriate, the resolution of monitoring procedures. In addition, a new system of substitutions is being created, in order to meet the high interinity, and the disciplinary regime of the future lawyers of the Administration of Justice is also being improved. Members of the procedural management body are recognized as agents of the authority to document embargoes or launches and the door is opened for psychologists and social workers to be part of the forensic comprehensive assessment units in the institutes of forensic medicine and sciences, with the aim of providing specialized assistance to victims of domestic violence, minors, families and persons with disabilities. With regard to social graduates, they are included as professionals who can act as collaborators of justice in the social sphere. A fifth aspect that I would like to highlight in this reform, which seems very relevant to me, is that of improving the legal and forensic medicine system, establishing, among other measures, that to access the body of forensic doctors will require the specialty in Legal Medicine, via MIR and with a subsequent opposition for its entry. The draft provides for the possibility for forensics to carry out expertise at the request of individuals in procedures that are not necessarily judicial, but this is in line with the Government's decision to establish alternative dispute resolution mechanisms in which forensics can be very useful collaborators. After the decriminalization of the faults, we are working on the regulation of an out-of-court complaint procedure for the claim of compensation for traffic accidents, allowing the person concerned to use, as so far, the reports of forensic doctors. Finally, there are new measures to combat gender-based violence and to do so, the competence of the judge of violence against women is expanded, the forensic comprehensive assessment units are strengthened and specialized training is provided in this area by all legal operators. These are, ladies and gentlemen, the basic lines of the project. Mentioning at least some of the criticisms that have been made, I would like to respond to some aspects. For example, we are being called upon to take advantage of this initiative to decentralize the Government of the Judiciary through the creation of the Justice Councils of the Autonomous Communities. It is true that the eventual regulation of such councils is an option and not an obligation, as the Constitutional Court said, in its judgement 31/2010 of 28 June. We are reminded that the judiciary cannot have any more governing body than the General Council of the Judiciary, whose status and functions are expressly reserved for the organic legislator. Furthermore, we believe, ladies and gentlemen, that we are not facing a demand from all citizens. The Justice Councils, ultimately, cannot be governing bodies of the judiciary and therefore cannot have jurisdiction over appointments, promotions, inspection or disciplinary regimes. With regard to the transfer of powers to the autonomous communities so that they can assume the management of the bodies of judicial secretaries or other personnel in the service of the Administration of Justice, I must recall that they are bodies of national scope, which guarantees them greater homogeneity in judicial proceedings, which in turn results in greater efficiency of justice and a better safeguard of the right to equality of all Spaniards, and there is abundant jurisprudence on this matter. This does not rule out that we can work with the autonomous communities that have assumed the competences in personal and material means, to expand their competences and to reorder in the future the Staff Regulations of the officials of the Administration of Justice. I'm done, ladies and gentlemen. I do so by insisting on the enormous possibilities that always involve a gradual, progressive and constant strategy. This is the case of a reform that, like this one, deepens some of the potentialities so far little exploited by the Organic Law of the Judiciary, which learns from the experience derived from its application and renews it, providing it with very valuable means to instill solidity, certainty and agility in our judicial organization. None of this means granting that the measures envisaged in these two bills are, as some have described it, a mere conglomerate. Far from that, they set a number of priorities for achieving justice close to the citizens, sensitive to their needs and typical of the twenty-first century. The speeding up and specialization of judicial responses, the improvement in the quality of resolutions, the professionalization of professional bodies, the fight against gender-based violence, among many other measures, are included in the projects I have just presented. I invite your ladies and gentlemen to reconsider whether these are not objectives on which we can all find ourselves. I personally believe that this can be done, and I am sure that with the work of the Committee on Justice we will be able to achieve these shared objectives among all. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u157	Thank you, Mr. Llamazares. For the Socialist Group, Mr Such.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u158	Thank you, Madam President. They bring us one more patch to modify certain very specific aspects of a law that is centennial and that would be worth analysing in its entirety or in a broader way to see if we can address a real justice reform and, above all, in the civil process, so that we reduce the costs to citizens and we can be more effective, more agile and citizens feel better represented. No, you bring this reform which is a patch, it is a retal. Funny thing is, to bring a patch, we need to know what's stuck so we can plug it in. And you say: there are three or four points that legal operators are asking us to change. We have held meetings with legal operators, lawyers and all over the world, and from what you contribute in amending the Civil Prosecution Act there is nothing urgent that implies a need for, through a patch, the Civil Prosecution Act to be amended. Moreover, it goes contrary to reason. You are aware that not long ago there was talk about the functions of prosecutors as far as the procedure is concerned. Of course, prosecutors are seen by citizens not as those who represent them, but as those who participate in the procedure, cost them money and do not provide legal solutions to the process. He is the legal representative of the citizen before the procedure. All right. The normal trend would be to eliminate the competencies and functions and to convert the body of prosecutors into another fundamental aspect - they were already predicting this situation - as it is to be able to open offices of lawyers. This provision has already been made, Minister, and you are now disembarking in this amendment in which you turn to the sock and say that the prosecutors are now going to have greater functions, you are going to be collaborators of the Administration of Justice and you are going to have functions of notification, legal aid and certification. They're going the other way. You know that the European Union is insisting, through the various bus laws, on the need to limit bureaucracy and reduce costs in procedures. And what you're doing here today, all you're going to do is cost the procedure, it's going to cost it more, and it's going to cost it in an important way. It is true - and other comrades have said - that here there is a double speed mounted. Anyone who has the resources to be able to pay the prosecutor to be his or her trusted attorney when making debt notifications or whatever, will be much more agile in his or her participation in the judicial process. However, those who do not have the resources will have to go to the judicial office and will have to enable the normal mechanisms that the Administration of Justice carries out in this case. There is a problem that has been communicated to us by legal operators, in this case lawyers and other associations. On the issue of prosecutors, the concern is that by being a party to the proceedings, it can really harm effective judicial guardianship because it is going to carry out acts with the defendant or the opposite party; they are going to be acts that will hardly be able to be controlled by the judicial secretary, because they can be massive acts of communication or judicial assistance or certification. We can think that a company that has its office of procurators to tutiplen, to full dedication, will be able to speed up its procedures in an important way, and this will really harm effective judicial guardianship because they are party and affect the rights of the other party, of the defendant in this case. This will be difficult to take up in the concept of effective judicial guardianship and, above all, the improvement of justice in general. This is a very complicated issue that many groups have raised with us. Minister, on the subject of the oral trial, Law 1/2007, on Civil Prosecution, establishes in its preamble that an oral trial is necessary, rapid, agile, in which it has seen, in which the parties see their faces and can contradict themselves in their positions, and establishes such a procedural act as the hearing of the oral trial and exalts it, and we understand that orality, agility, speed in the discussion of the claims of the parties is fundamental. However, they bring us here a return to the past, which means that the oral trial is going to have a written answer from the defendant, apologizing, moreover, for the need for the actor to know the evidence provided by the defendant, as you said in your intervention. Well, the concept of orality, immediacy, speed, procedural agility through verbal judgment will carry it with all the consequences. The explanatory statement said so. The law designed the declaratory processes so that immediacy, publicity and orality would be effective, and this was reflected in the verbal judgments because of the importance of the hearing, as well as in the ordinary, Minister, by the prior hearing and by the trial itself, and in both cases with the presence of the judge. The change in your verbal judgment is a very important step backwards. They also modify the limitation of personal actions, which goes from fifteen to five years, with the obligation that the party acting, in this case the creditor, have to file the claim in one year and, therefore, as much as it would be six years, because it would paralyze the limitation. Moreover, they do so - you have said - to promote the development of the Second Opportunity Law. There are many collectives - get on the Internet and access the legal pages - who have worked a lot on this subject, and the truth is that they are shouting the sky, especially because also the late professionals will benefit from the modification of the prescription of the personal actions that you are putting forward. Let us rethink this amendment that you propose, to see if it can be improved in the discussion of the law. Another element they raise is the procedural succession in execution, a succession that occurs without being able to express an opinion on the part of the affected person. This project clearly seeks to promote the presence of certain funds, vulture funds, and the procedural succession of entities that only seek to obtain small profits. Your proposal for reform clearly favours you, Minister. I would also like to add a few words about how you adapt European legislation. The adaptation to the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in June 2012 is two years late, in a limited way and with a complex and faracious procedure, which almost turns into a declaratory judgment and which does not properly comply with the judgment. I would also like to inform you that, in the course of partial amendments, we will present you with a proposal to improve the procedure and to extend it to mortgage and monetary executions. On the subject of the compulsory use of telematic communications, of 1 January 2016, Minister, we must hurry; we must hurry to put order in this situation, because - I doubt it just as before my colleagues - on 1 January 2016 it will be practically impossible for the use of telematic means to be in place, in operation the obligatory use of telematic means when making communications, the transfer of writings and documents, especially because autonomous powers are also being invaded and will have to be brought into line with all the public administrations affected by this matter. We reiterate our group's willingness to discuss everything, to bring modern, viable, transformative proposals, as well as our deep rejection of the fact that our legal system is subject to unrelated changes, to permanent and contradictory patches in the various laws that you have presented to us recently. Finally, we propose the return of this project to the Government, because the reform it contains is unnecessary insofar as it does not serve what should be its objectives, which are: to improve public service and the response to citizenship by keeping it in exclusively public hands. This reform does not respond to an urgent demand from society, Minister, but to the government's need to facilitate the privatization of basic aspects of civil procedure, which, as I said earlier, entails a slower, more unjust and less subject to the principles of the constitutions governing civil procedure. We therefore call for the House to vote in favour of the full amendment of the Government's bill in the defence of the public service. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-16-CD150416.u166	Minister, ladies and gentlemen, I rise to this rostrum to set out the position of the People's Parliamentary Group on the amendments to the entire bill to amend the Civil Prosecution Act. Minister, you have explained the bill in detail and I will try to explain why we are going to oppose these amendments in full. I will start by bringing up some of the arguments that the proposing groups are using to justify their amendments. Sincerely, I have been drawn to your arguments, even this very afternoon I have been amazed by some interventions. The proposers say that this reform involves a privatization of justice and to justify it the Socialist Group says: This reform is a further concession to the prosecutors, to whom it deeply dislikes to see the justiciable and even the lawyers, and who regard the view as unnecessary. I think it is an unfortunate and unfair expression, just as it is treated in this way to a collective of this prestige, as it is that of the Spanish prosecutors. [[Applauds]] With all due respect, I think you should explain to those professionals why you have such animosity towards the prosecutors, because this very afternoon I also heard Mr Such say the same thing about the prosecutors. The People's Parliamentary Group does not have that view. I can assure you that we deserve respect and admiration as a collective. [[Applauds]] I find the expression of the Plural Left captivating when to justify an amendment states that a two-speed justice will be installed or, as Mr Tardà has said, a justice for the rich and another for the poor. It is captivating because it introduces a demagogic message to confuse public opinion and also questions the effectiveness of the Public Service vis-à-vis the private. I would ask you, Mr. Llamazares: do you really think that the officials of the Administration of Justice are less effective, less efficient and less agile than the prosecutors? They'll have to explain why they think that way too. I ask you to explain your position to these officials. With regard to the amendment of the whole Republican Left, I am amazed and puzzled and I say to you why. It seems to me to be an unscrupulous amendment and it is a direct mistake to present an amendment to the whole on the basis of which the bill is exceeded by granting prosecutors tasks of judicial execution. Mr. Tardà, the bill only grants acts of communication, but it does not grant acts of embargo or acts of execution. Consequently, I do not understand which bill has been read. I doubt that the amendment they have tabled has sufficient parliamentary rigour and legal rigour to take it into consideration. Ladies and gentlemen, if I told you that this reform is a recommendation of the General Council of the Judiciary in the White Paper on Justice, would you continue to maintain that judges are trying to introduce a two-speed justice or that the objective of judges is to privatize justice? It comes in the white book itself. Ladies and gentlemen, the aim of the reform is to extend and also to clarify the functions of prosecutors within the text of the Civil Prosecution Act and in Act No. 37/2011 of 10 October 2011 on procedural expediting measures in relation to communication acts. This reform is part of the already existing duality of a system that maintains the possibility that the acts of communication may be carried out either by the services of officials seconded to the Administration of Justice or by the prosecutor, under the responsibility of the party, and it will be the judicial secretary who controls, supervises and directs the procedural acts. The reform is therefore not a privatization of the service, but an option with which the citizen has access and assistance and collaboration in the service of the Administration of Justice, as has been interpreted by the Council of State. There is also no justification for the Socialist Group's amendment on the reform of the Minutes. Ladies and gentlemen, the process of replying to the complaint does not violate the principle of immediacy, but rather strengthens the principle of effective judicial protection by preventing the defence of the plaintiff in the face of allegations or evidence that are surprisingly made in the oral proceedings. With regard to the complaint of delay in the proceedings that Mr Such has said, I would remind you that it is the view of the General Council of the Judiciary and the Council of State that effective judicial protection must prevail over the agility of the proceedings. There is therefore no basis for the amendment that you are submitting. It is a step towards speeding up the procedure rather than delaying the possibility that the parties may resign from the hearing when the outcome of the proceedings depends on questions of law or when the evidence is unnecessary, without forgetting that this reform of the oral trial is also positively endorsed by the various legal operators. With regard to the reform of the monitoring process, we cannot agree with the particulars of the amendment, firstly because the reform is meeting an obligation to adapt Spanish legislation to European law and secondly because it would be unfair not to protect the consumer from the so-called unfair clauses in the monitoring procedures. I would remind you again that this reform is also supported by the Council of State. I shall conclude, ladies and gentlemen, not without making a brief reference to the statute of limitations. The statute of limitations in the reform of the Civil Procedure Act, on the one hand, is a constant demand by the General Council of the Judiciary to simplify and unify the statute of limitations and, on the other, seeks to regulate that extrajudicial claims may delay the statute of limitations, but do not last forever, thus avoiding what is known as the theory of unfair delay that has caused the debtors so much damage. Finally, I would like to thank the Minister of Justice for the positive attitude of consensus and for the dialogue that the parliamentary groups can maintain during the reform process with the amendments to the joint text that they present. Thank you, Mr. President. [[Applauds]]
